The sound of flesh clumping together, the sound of grinding, wailing, footsteps....

“Sw-swollen—hiiik!”

The hair on the back of my neck stood on end.

Don’t look back.

I had to get away. I had to get away...!!

My survival instinct shoved at my back. I ran without even breathing.

In the department store glass to my left as I flashed past, a child holding their parents’ hands was staring with curious eyes.

My breath caught in my throat.

Beeeeeeep!

[Se-gwang Special City]

Not a disaster alert join the sa-sa-san-san-walk event let’s walk together

To paradise

The alert sound rang from everywhere. I stuffed my phone away without reading more.

No—why did I even pull it out and read it while running for my life... wait.

...Why can I see the department store doors again?

I was running the wrong way.

Somehow, I had turned around again and was running toward City Hall.

...The department store glass doors were on my right.

“Uhh—?”

A slow, steady hum was coming out of my mouth—

No.

Someone shoved past me and bolted forward.

“Hk.”

The humming that had been coming out of my mouth cut off for an instant. Fear, confusion, panic—spreading everywhere like an infection. I made a snap judgment.

I have to get away.

I have to get off the street!

I whipped around and sprinted back into the department store.

“Waaah!”

“Ah!”

“Close the doors!”

Behind me, I heard the glass doors slam shut—and something slapping wetly against the glass.

Without hesitating, I tore across the entire first-floor area like I’d lost my mind, sprinting for the exit on the opposite side.

“Ah!”

“What is it?”

People who still didn’t grasp how serious it was glanced over, then noticed the commotion in the lobby and screamed.

Leaving behind the disaster alert notification sound starting up again in the lobby, I kept running.

Soon, an outdoor cargo parking lot came into view.

“Haa....”

I got out of the department store.

Blue sky, sunlight—the outdoor parking lot looked peaceful, but I could hear screams and bursting sounds echoing from over there.

I looked at my phone, now gone quiet.

...If you get within a certain radius of that, does the sound start?

Damn it, damn it!

Why did I drop into the morning of disaster day all of a sudden?

My head swam.

What am I supposed to do?

Se-gwang Special City is going to collapse now—how do I survive? No, first I have to find where Section Chief Lee Jaheon is, and the two people who were inside the tattoo....

[Oh my, friend. The more you rush, the more you end up wasting. Don’t waste your precious ideas and thoughts—let’s think efficiently....]

Right!

It felt like sparks were flying through my head.

I know Se-gwang Special City.

The geography, locations, transit here—things I naturally learned by repeating a peaceful May 4 more than six hundred times.

And the past few months, too, spent exploring beneath Se-gwang Special City after it became a catastrophic disaster.

I know what’s where in Se-gwang Special City, and how all the landmarks in this special city mutated after disaster day.

If I infer backward...

If this insane May 4 is repeating endlessly in Se-gwang Special City, then I have to figure out a way out—somehow.

Search and escape.

I decided.

I moved, breathing deep.

After some thought, I didn’t turn my phone off. It was also a notice that the “disaster phenomenon” had gotten close.

For now, I have to get away from Se-gwang City Hall, which looks like the origin point of the disaster.

—The teachers hurriedly collected all our phones. They cut all the TV cords.

And they told them to close their eyes and cover their ears....

The Counseling Teacher.

I would be able to meet Hoyuwon as well.

[You’ve chosen a destination, friend.]

The bus won’t work.

Following the street would be a stupid move, and by now the roads would be chaos.

Wait.

—A bicycle rack

In that corner, six or seven bikes were chained up....

And so, a short while later.

I’m sorry...!

I was riding a bicycle.

I picked the oldest, dustiest one—the one whose owner clearly hadn’t come to retrieve it—so at least it wouldn’t be a tragedy where the owner got it stolen while trying to flee and stood there in stunned despair!

I have to stick to alleys.

Avoiding people, I started biking toward the high school through small streets and between shops instead of the main road.

“What is it? What is it?”

“Hey—cut into the café street!”

My bike, deft, squeezed through narrow passages, dodging panicked people.

My bicycle-riding skills, trained in the Disaster Management Bureau, were shining in the weirdest place.

No—come to think of it, I’m going to find people in a supernatural disaster, so maybe it’s exactly the right time for it to—

“Aaaagh!”

I snapped my head around.

Right ahead—people poured out of the side path next to a convenience store in the alley and toppled in a heap right in front of my bike.

Damn it!

I barely vaulted over them, almost going down myself.

It looked like five or six people had fallen. I shouted while fighting to steady the bike.

“Are you all right?”

It seemed like they’d been running through the alley, someone rounded a corner, fell, shoved into others, and they all tangled and went down together.

One of the fallen people jerked upright without answering and started running.

“Go the opposite direction from City Hall! Avoid the main roads!”

“Get up!”

The others shoved and hauled each other up and began running too, but one middle-aged person couldn’t get up.

“Ugh....”

A high school student who’d been pinned at the very bottom was instead helping that middle-aged person to their feet.

Damn it, damn it.

Someone was standing in the side path.

The shape of a person holding a phone.

A Se-gwang Special City citizen dressed ordinarily, wearing a flower-patterned dress.

Ah.

They were swelling.

I could hear humming.

The melody was becoming more and more specific.

Letting out a soft humming tone, they staggered as they walked toward this way....

The disaster alert rang on my phone, too.

Ha.

The hair on my scalp prickled. Climbing off the saddle, I shoved the bike forward toward the alley where it was.

“Go!”

The middle-aged person, finally upright with the high school student’s help, ran off in a scramble.

I wanted to scream and run, too.

It’s already pushing into the alleys.

It was lucky there weren’t many of them.

Get out fast.

I gritted my teeth and tried to run again. I planned to use my feet for now, then steal a bike again when I found one.

The high school student who’d helped the middle-aged person was heading toward where I’d shoved the bike.

“...!”

I grabbed the student and yanked them back, and I kicked the bike away again.

The student stumbled along half-dragged, then started running on their own.

“Haa....”

“Are you alri—”

“......”

The uniform this high school student was wearing was one I recognized.

Se-gwang Tech...?

And stopped completely.

“Huff... y-yes, thank you....”

Bronze Agent Ryu Jaegwan—wearing the body of a high school student—was panting for breath.

Chapter 337

Bronze Agent...

Why is he wearing Se-gwang Tech’s uniform, looking like a child?

“......”

My mind almost went blank from this insane situation, but I forced myself to steady my head and start reading what was happening.

Bronze Agent looked a little younger even than the high schooler appearance I’d seen in the “In the Black Shade” urban legend.

How do I persuade them?

...Bronze Agent said the first time he got dragged into an urban legend and was rescued by the Disaster Management Bureau, he was a student.

High school student Ryu Jaegwan stared at me with trembling eyes, then gave a firm nod.

Seeing him grit his teeth and keep up, it looked like Bronze Agent’s bicycle-riding talent had been promising from the start....

Skirting the disaster alert sounds and screams coming from all directions, I kept detouring and biking toward Se-gwang Technical High School.

But even those danger signals would vanish soon.

...Once there were no survivors left in the area, the alerts would stop ringing on their own.

Move fast.

A convenience store.

“Wait.”

Mostly food.

Instead, after some thought, I bought two long-handled umbrellas that looked the sturdiest, and a tool set.

And to the cashier, a part-timer who’d been glancing at a book with earbuds in the whole time I paid, I said,

“Get out of here quickly.”

“A bio-chemical terror incident happened near City Hall and it’s spreading. Run to a subway station as fast as you can.”

“!!”

Only then did the part-timer turn on their phone and check their messages, their face going pale as they started frantically scrolling.

Outside, I looked at high school student Ryu Jaegwan, standing awkwardly with his hands on his bike, watching me.

“Open your bag.”

“Yes?”

I put two small bottles of water and calorie bars into his schoolbag. Then I handed him an umbrella and had him strap it along the bike.

At that roundabout answer, I realized his assigned background here would probably be close to reality.

—His parents divorced when he was young, and after his mother passed away, he grew up in a group home.

...Let’s not talk about it any further.

I rode on in silence again.

Maybe because he was built well, but even after that, Ryu Jaegwan kept up without trouble.

Good.

Soon, Se-gwang Technical High School’s wall came into view.

I answered without delay, quietly scanning the surroundings as I stepped inside the grounds.

When I parked the bike in an inconspicuous corner, Ryu Jaegwan copied me, leaving his bike and following after.

...The Security Guard isn’t coming out.

It was strange.

Like something was jammed.

Huh?

A window that looked into the first-floor corridor.

Exactly.

I saw students lying down in the corridor, asleep.

It was the same scene as the peaceful ending credits I’d seen after clearing the “In the Black Shade” urban legend.

Seeing talismans stuck into the gaps of every classroom door and window, I felt a strange aftertaste.

So the barrier Team Leader Hong set... manifested like this.

Keeping the children asleep even through the repeating disaster day.

Maybe that comatose state was judged as a kind of death—like the Baekseolsan apple I’d eaten—and they were isolated from the catastrophic disaster that spread through disaster alerts.

So we can’t open the school door.

It probably wouldn’t open easily anyway.

At the pattern Bronze Agent had drawn himself.

“.......”

Well, it took me over six hundred repeats to get out—expecting Bronze Agent to snap out of it from a few words was absurd.

If anything, I’d just look suspicious, pointing at talismans and saying things.

Don’t get impatient.

In that case—

I smiled at high school student Ryu Jaegwan.

Right. Hoyuwon.

If the school doors are locked and everyone’s asleep, how can the Counseling Teacher come out?

Ryu Jaegwan’s and my disaster alerts rang at the same time.

...Near the gate.

In the shadow at the corner of the school building, a smiling Security Guard was watching us, phone in one hand.

They’d fused with another Security Guard.

Se-gwang citizens who looked like residents from the nearby apartments, wearing calm expressions.

Part of that wave walking along the main road was visible right outside the gate....

The Security Guards were walking across the field.

Waiting for the Counseling Teacher... we give that up for now.

Don’t let that get into the school building.

The instant I moved my foot.

The Counseling Teacher.

No.

The Counseling Teacher isn’t human.

A cooperative spirit.

Meaning—this was an urban legend, too.

Was melting, sloughing down.

My head swayed.

Please.

Director Ho’s true form.

Splat.

As if there were no pain at all.

Like it had never been there.

Like it was being deleted.

Maybe because he wasn’t human.

Ryu Jaegwan.

How to bring Ryu Jaegwan with me.

“You saw it earlier, right? The disaster alerts, the monsters, the strange phenomena. Things like urban legends.”

“.......”

High school student Ryu Jaegwan nodded.

I continued, steady.

“This is a disaster called a supernatural phenomenon. I’m an agent of a government agency that handles these phenomena—the Supernatural Disaster Management Bureau.”

“...!”

“I came here because there are people I absolutely have to rescue....”

Looking at Ryu Jaegwan, whose eyes were shining without him realizing it, I said,

“Do you know who one of them is?”

“...The Counseling Teacher?”

“No.”

Smiling, I looked at the high school student.

“It’s you.”

“...!”

“Student Ryu Jaegwan.”

Hearing a stranger call his name—his name I’d never told him—high school student Ryu Jaegwan’s eyes wavered.

“It’s a good thing I found you quickly. So....”

Gripping him, I said,

“Until Student Ryu Jaegwan safely escapes this disaster, I will stay with you.”

Chapter 338

A mountain trail in Se-gwang Special City.

Ryu Jaegwan, gasping for breath, looked at the person running ahead.

The person on the bicycle—no, the “agent”—was making astonishingly fast, decisive calls and leading him along.

“Huff....”

They were riding the mountain now.

More precisely, they were taking a shortcut out of the residential district by using a well-maintained mountain walking trail to avoid densely populated areas.

[Walking Trail & Rest Area]

It was maintained just enough that they didn’t quite need a mountain bike.

On the lead agent’s back, the Counseling Teacher was perched as if laid over him, and Ryu Jaegwan was clinging on behind with a bike he still wasn’t used to, sheer stubbornness forcing him forward.

The agent kept checking his own condition without slowing down. You could feel the will in him—get away from danger as fast as possible.

Even in this situation that was like a disaster movie—so cold it felt unreal, so urgent it made your skin crawl—he was calm and resolute.

And yet....

“Student Jaegwan, careful!”

How does he know me?

I’m an orphan with no connections.

Then why is he trying to rescue me?

Something that wasn’t quite hope and wasn’t quite fear churned in my chest. Between the terror of an extreme, non-everyday situation where I might die, a strange thrill rose up.

A teenager’s longing.

The excitement of being given a special role.

But things were too urgent for that feeling to take the front.

Beeeep!

“...!”

“We’re turning!”

The moment the disaster alert alarm rang again, the bike in front swerved hard.

Ryu Jaegwan hurried after it.

But a shiver ran through him at the realization that he’d almost let go of the handlebars, grabbed his phone, and checked the message.

That impulse was getting worse and worse....

And after riding for who knew how long—

“Over there—huff—let’s rest for a moment.”

They stopped in front of a rest area sign.

Ryu Jaegwan reflexively swept his gaze across the inside.

The rest area halfway up the mountain trail was a cabin, with a small flat clearing around it and a fence set up.

In a corner, in a gap between rocks, he could see a faucet shaped like a wooden sculpture and, beneath it, pooled water like a little spring.

[Comma Mineral Spring (No Washing Allowed)]

Following the agent, Ryu Jaegwan got off the saddle and wheeled his bike by hand, trailing toward the cabin entrance....

He heard the Counseling Teacher whisper softly.

“There are other people inside the cabin.”

...!

“They’re watching us from the window. ...I don’t think their intentions are good. Don’t look.”

Ryu Jaegwan almost turned toward the window. Cold sweat slid down his temples.

...He could almost feel a leering gaze scanning their bicycles, their bodies, their supplies.

Then, beside him, he heard a flat voice catching its breath.

“We weren’t going to go in anyway.... If we need to move right away, indoors isn’t good. Let’s just sit outside the rest area for a moment and then go. There’s a spring here.”

At the agent’s calm voice, the teenager’s prickling tension eased.

“Are you all right, Student Jaegwan?”

“......Yes.”

After checking Ryu Jaegwan’s condition with a faint smile, the two adults’ faces hardened and they began speaking in low voices.

“It’s only been an hour since the disaster started, and people are already thinking in extremes....”

“When it’s your life on the line, nothing’s off the table. They must’ve realized right away this isn’t normal. It’s only natural.”

They were talking about the “people with bad intentions” inside the cabin.

Ryu Jaegwan swallowed.

But the agent who looked back at him seemed unfazed, just patting the seat beside him as if to say It’s fine.

His eyes between black hair were even.

As if he’d lived through this countless times.

Ryu Jaegwan, who had no idea what kind of internal scream this “agent” might be holding back, felt a small measure of relief and sat beside him.

Then, catching their breath, they traded words about the situation.

“The alarm that rang earlier... it probably wasn’t on the mountain trail. It was likely down below. We should be safe here for a while. We don’t know when it’ll happen again, so let’s take in water and nutrition now.”

The agent pulled out an empty plastic bottle from his pack, filled it at the spring, and held it out to the Counseling Teacher.

The Counseling Teacher refused, but under the agent’s insistence, he drank a sip or two and put a candy in his mouth.

Then the agent asked him.

“You said you’re going to the station.”

“Yes.”

“You said there’s a way to end this disaster... so exactly where are you trying to go by subway?”

“I’m going to Se-gwang Station.”

At that, agitation showed on the agent’s previously calm face.

Why?

“Teacher. The origin point of this disaster is Se-gwang City Hall. Se-gwang Station is right near there.”

Ah.

“What you’re saying is you’re going to step into the fire while it’s burning. With no manual, no basic equipment, in a newly erupted large-scale disaster.”

Then the agent paused, swallowed, and spoke.

Ryu Jaegwan had the strange sense it was—somewhat deliberately—meant to heighten urgency.

“And this isn’t an ordinary disaster. I... at minimum this is wave-class. In the worst case....”

“I know.”

The Counseling Teacher’s voice sounded slightly anxious.

“That’s why we have to move faster. And don’t worry. I have my own methods.”

Then the Counseling Teacher suddenly thrust out an empty candy wrapper, almost as if to show them.

He clenched his hand tight so they couldn’t see inside, then opened it.

And—

Sparkle.

From inside the candy wrapper, small red bead-like things spilled into his palm.

“...!!”

“Looks like magic, doesn’t it?”

They were small berries, ripened bright red.

“They’re the fruit of a plant called fox marbles, Student Jaegwan.”

Speaking with a hint of warmth, the Counseling Teacher held the tiny fruit and smiled in a slightly playful way.

“If you eat this, you’ll be fine for a while. You won’t be bewitched by the disaster alerts. ...I can’t promise you’ll be completely safe, so we still need to get out of here as fast as possible.”

As he spoke, the Counseling Teacher’s eyes dimmed for a moment, as if looking at the empty space where his right hand had been—slack and melted, then cut off—but he smiled again and held out the berries.

“Go on.”

Ryu Jaegwan hesitated, watching the agent. When the agent nodded, he took the berries.

He’d thought it earlier—when the Counseling Teacher shoved his hand into the Security Guard-turned-monster, and when they cut that hand off—

The Counseling Teacher didn’t seem like an ordinary person....

.......

Wait—did his school even have a Counseling Teacher like that?

...Was he always like that?

A faint sense of wrongness rose up, but a young, restless mind quickly erased it.

Because the Counseling Teacher’s next words kept coming.

“So you two eat this and go somewhere safe.”

The agent closed his mouth.

Ryu Jaegwan studied his face.

For an instant, he felt fierce deliberation and conflict flicker across it.

“Thank you.”

A composed voice followed.

“Student Jaegwan.”

“...Yes?”

“If we just take him to the station, then we can keep following the mountain trail like this. We’ll reach a river. There won’t be many people there, so it’ll be relatively safe.”

When their eyes met, the agent spoke gently.

“We can use that route to get out of this city. And I’ll stay with you until you get out of this disaster safely, so don’t worry.”

“.......”

In that moment, Ryu Jaegwan understood.

Right now, this agent had given something up for him.

“For now, that’s the plan.”

Ryu Jaegwan spoke up.

Ryu Jaegwan said it steadily.

That was clearly what weighed on the agent.

When the agent’s complexion changed, Ryu Jaegwan felt relief instead.

Resolve beat fear.

“This is an urgent situation. Until you find the others you need to rescue, you don’t have to take care of me separately. I’m... strong, and I can do what you tell me without complaining.”

“No—”

The agent’s face hardened, then he let out a breath. Ryu Jaegwan’s heart lurched, but the agent looked at him with eyes that said, somehow, I knew you’d say that.

...How?

“First—”

The moment the agent began to speak—

“Hey!”

...!

At the unfamiliar voice, they turned.

...Between the cabin door, cracked open only halfway, a pair of eyes appeared.

A chill shot through Ryu Jaegwan as he sprang to his feet.

“You’re not coming in?”

The door opened wider, and a middle-aged man in ordinary hiking clothes stepped out.

“Outside doesn’t look normal. Come in for now! Even a little safer is better.”

Ah.

He was trying to help.

For a moment, the high schooler relaxed.

But the two adults shoved the high schooler behind them and took on a strange, guarded posture.

‘...!’

“It’s all right. We’re going to head out again.”

“Head out? They say terror happened at City Hall—are you going to a shelter?”

Then he glanced at the bikes.

“Riding that’ll be fast, sure, but wouldn’t it be better to eat something first? Take at least a cup ramen. We’ve got some inside.”

But while his words pretended concern, the eyes that naturally slid to the bicycles were greedy.

So Ryu Jaegwan understood.

He wants the bikes.

When the roads are blocked, there’s no better means of moving.

As alarm flared in him and he reflexively opened his mouth—

“Agent....”

“Agent?”

The middle-aged man’s eyes swept the agent’s jacket. It looked like he realized it was a uniform; his expression changed.

“Uh—are you a civil servant?”

Damn.

“Police? Military?”

Inside the cabin, he could hear other people standing up, a rising murmur.

Ryu Jaegwan clenched his teeth, but the agent smiled.

“Mm. It’s similar, but... we’re not on duty. We don’t have support from our organization, either.”

Then he patted Ryu Jaegwan on the back.

“I’m just traveling with my younger brother.”

...!

The two adults eased backward, then climbed onto their bikes.

“Jaegwan!”

Ryu Jaegwan grabbed his bike without hesitation and started pedaling like crazy.

“Hey!”

Glancing back, he saw people bursting out of the cabin door, holding trekking poles and what looked like wire cords.

A chill hit him and he pedaled harder.

Soon, the footsteps behind them faded.

“Student Jaegwan, the berries!”

“Ah...!”

Heart hammering, Ryu Jaegwan shoved the berries—nearly crushed in his grip—into his mouth.

Somehow, even mixed with fear and revulsion, he felt a faint sense of brightness.

A sense of belonging.

Holy shit, I almost got killed.

Tongue out, I pumped my legs harder.

Those people were absolutely about to tie us up and steal the bikes.

[We nearly screened a low-budget thriller film script this afternoon. How about the title ‘The Cabin Murder Case’? Something obvious and vulgar would suit it perfectly!]

Hahaha....

Hoo.

From behind my head, I heard the Counseling Teacher speak.

“You’re a flexible agent. Some agents I know might’ve tried to persuade those people too, to rescue them somehow.”

Ah.

Just what did it take for this person to get picked by the Disaster Management Bureau in a way that set it off like a rabid button....

This is driving me insane.

Is it really right to take him to the subway station like this?

It wasn’t bitterness anymore—it was almost despair. Holding back a sigh, I pedaled again.

The Counseling Teacher hanging off my back was strangely light, and the bike whipped forward, closing in on the station.

Jui Station

The station name was different from the mutated subway station I’d experienced.

But I could guess, roughly.

Near the station, I could see a massive local broadcasting station building.

Ohu Station (Blood Broadcasting Station)

A subway station connected to the Se-gwang Special City Local Broadcasting Station.

In this station, a temporary stage had been installed in the concourse in coordination with the broadcasting station, and civic choirs or orchestras sometimes performed there.

I could vividly recall the wiki entry I’d read back then.

This is almost the exact opposite position from Se-gwang Station.

Thankfully, because it was far away, the commotion still hadn’t reached the level of the disaster’s origin point. Those “entities” walking along the main road from City Hall hadn’t arrived here yet.

Good call taking the mountain shortcut by bike.

But the area around it was already desolate, as if everyone had evacuated.

There were barely any pedestrians on the street—just a few moving fast, as if fleeing.

“.......”

And as I looked at Ryu Jaegwan, wearing a stubborn expression that said I’m absolutely not leaving alone, I swallowed a sigh.

This one’s driving me insane too.

Let’s think while we see the Counseling Teacher into the station.

Inside the subway, at least, it was a space where the disaster outside couldn’t easily reach—so there was a decent chance Se-gwang Station would be relatively intact.

It might even be safer than when I visited.

Whether we can get out of this city safely is a problem too, so confirming more information before moving wouldn’t be the worst idea.

I forced down the anxiety.

Hoo.

First, right in front of the station entrance, I stopped and collected the bicycles.

“Hand them over.”

And I shoved both bikes straight into my tattoo.

“...!!”

High school student Bronze Agent nodded hard. His eyes were twice as wide now.

After checking the surroundings, I started heading down through Subway Exit 3.

Fortunately, the entrance hadn’t been barricaded yet.

“.......”

Going back into the subway felt strange.

And the subway I entered—

“What do we do??”

“My mom won’t answer the phone....”

“Aaaaaagh!”

I understood where all the people who’d vanished from the streets had gone.

“We’re all dead! They say it’s a virus terror attack!”

“All paramedics dead—this is real?”

“This video—look at that, is it real? Is this really happening at City Hall?”

Citizens who’d swarmed into the subway station as a shelter packed the concourse wall-to-wall.

On top of that, panicked testimony from people who’d fled from other stations turned it into a madhouse.

We’re already infected from the bio-chemical terror, war broke out and nuclear missiles will be launched soon—rumors of every kind made the panic surge.

In one corner, people were fighting like they meant to kill, and others were trying to pull them apart.

Somebody else wandered barefoot, muttering to themself....

Damn it.

It was the kind of scene that made your spine go cold.

We hurried, trying to avoid the crowd and get down to the platform.

“What are you going to do?”

“For now, I’m thinking of taking the outer loop line—”

But the platform toward the outer loop line was already blocked.

...Just like when I’d explored the subway stations.

[Oh? So it was already impossible from day one!]

“No service! Go back!”

Station staff stood in the way, controlling the crowd. Damn it!

We sprinted back to the opposite platform.

“It’s a loop line anyway, so taking the other side is fine. It’ll be more trouble, but....”

“.......”

I realized something.

Taking the inner loop gets you to Se-gwang Station a tiny bit faster.

Because there are fewer stops.

So choosing the opposite direction on purpose means....

My gaze slid to the machinery room on the blocked platform beyond the tracks.

“Is the place you’re trying to go... a facility on the opposite platform?”

“......!”

The Pleasant Research Institute facility.

For an instant, an eerie, strange look surfaced in the Counseling Teacher’s eyes as he stared at me.

A gaze that observed.

“...Why do you think that?”

“.......”

That was when—

The train is arriving.

“...!”

A train began to enter the platform. Beyond the screen doors, it shoved air forward as it rushed in and stopped.

And then—

The screen doors are opening.

Some of the people crammed inside the train spilled out as if shoved. People near the doors struggled not to get out, but were pushed and tumbled anyway—

“Aaaagh!”

“Save me!”

The moment they got off the train, several people collapsed, covered in injuries.

Some clung to each other and sobbed, others barely managed to move.

It was pure chaos.

We have to get on in this?

“Teacher!”

Fortunately, the Counseling Teacher’s strange expression had vanished completely as he pushed through the crowd toward the train. I gritted my teeth, torn over whether to follow.

Or should I... check this station’s Pleasant Research Institute?

What would it even look like right now?

I looked at the opposite tracks, then at Ryu Jaegwan....

In that short moment, with the doors open, I made a fierce, rapid decision—

“Ggh....”

...I saw another group getting off the train.

And among them, there was a face I knew.

A cool, pale face, sharp lines.

Deputy Eun Haje.

“...!!”

“Agent...!”

Deputy Eun Haje—his hair longer than I remembered, tied tight into a single bundle—was being carried off the train on someone’s back, unconscious.

“Reporter!”

Faces that looked like coworkers were smeared with blood and sweat.

And I understood.

“Huuung....”

He wasn’t unconscious.

Eun Haje’s breath was already gone.

Chapter 339

I stopped breathing as I watched it.

Deputy Eun Haje’s people trying CPR.

His body shaking.

High school student Ryu Jaegwan, who’d been about to board the train, twisted around and started heading away from the train toward the concourse.

WoooOOOng—

He left.

Dodging evacuees sitting on the steps, sobbing or muttering, I climbed up—and saw figures clutching a body, not knowing what to do.

“Excuse me.”

I spoke to them directly. They jolted and turned to me.

“Yes??”

The form of address—

—Reporter!

The color drained from their tear-soaked faces, shifting.

I trailed off.

High school student Ryu Jaegwan stood beside me, and I heard him draw in a small, short breath as he looked at “Reporter Eun Haje’s” condition.

He noticed.

That it was a corpse.

I didn’t need to dress anything up. With a pale, bloodless face, I stared at Deputy Eun Haje and said,

“Why did this happen? What on earth happened?”

Then, as if they couldn’t hold it back anymore, testimony burst out of them in a flood.

“We—we... we were going to City Hall.”

“...!”

They said the cars ahead all stopped on the road like they’d been possessed.

Horn blares, people getting out and asking what was going on—

“We had a bad feeling, sure! They said terror, right? I mean, we were going thinking maybe it was just a mistaken alert, but... once that happened, it felt real.”

A shaking image.

—Hey, just run!

...Deputy Eun Haje’s voice.

Se-gwang City Hall in the upper-left corner of the screen, caught by the camera’s wild motion.

Everyone watching the footage was humming softly.

Even though I ate the berries the Counseling Teacher gave me.

While looking down at Deputy Eun Haje’s unfamiliar remains.

After showing the footage to a stranger, crying, spilling everything they’d been through, it looked like reason finally returned.

Carrying a body was brutally difficult, but it was also hard to decide to abandon it here.

What are you talking about.

To be honest, I want to wail too.

A way to save him.

Even if I put him into the tattoo and carry him around, a corpse is still a corpse.

If I die right now, would I wake up again in the morning of Se-gwang Special City’s disaster day, May 4?

Isn’t there another way?

A broadcasting station ID.

Blood Broadcasting Station....

Ohu Station.

Delusion Home Shopping.

And what moved into that home shopping’s place—

...The Flower Golden Resort pop-up.

.......

......!

Brown.

I swallowed.

Are the subway-station urban legends I experienced in Se-gwang Special City... the same space as this?

[Hm. Someone might say a calf and chateaubriand are the same thing, too. Isn’t everything just the difference of environment and conditions?]

It was a chilling metaphor, but I understood the ambiguity.

In the end, it depends on the viewer.

And in an urban legend, that kind of standard can form an important rule....

Then.

It’s worth trying.

“Student Jaegwan.”

Together with Ryu Jaegwan, I moved Deputy Eun Haje’s body into a corner.

Then I started using my hands.

“What... are you doing?”

“...You could call it emergency treatment.”

My hands were firm, but my head was a mess.

Isn’t this wrong?

But if the alternative is truly dying, isn’t this better? If the day really repeats anyway....

Questions and fear circled endlessly in my head, but my hands kept moving—desperate.

And a little later.

The dead Reporter Eun Haje opened his eyes.

“...!!”

“Hoo....”

Like waking from a deep sleep, he sat up, and high school student Ryu Jaegwan stared blankly at the sight.

At the reporter moving as if revived.

He furrowed his brow and looked at me with wary suspicion.

“And who the hell are you?”

Perfectly normal.

...Behind my back, I shoved the “Flower Golden Resort Employment Contract” deeper into my tattoo again.

It worked.

“So you’re telling me I just revived? Not three days, not three hours—thirty minutes?”

“...You simply came to your senses from a suspended state.”

“Then why doesn’t this hurt?”

“Adrenaline.”

“Well, that’s... a scientific explanation, sure.”

Deputy Eun Haje—no, “the reporter”—scowled, looking like he wanted a cigarette, eyes moving between me and Ryu Jaegwan.

He looked natural—human.

So Ryu Jaegwan kept flicking his gaze between me and Deputy Eun Haje with a confused expression like he had no idea what was happening.

And I was remembering the sloppy contract I’d written....

Day Labor Contract

Right.

To the “Delusion Home Shopping day-labor contract” I had for Deputy Eun Haje, I added a line.

Work as a Flower Golden Resort employee.

This employee is transferred under Flower Golden Resort.

Just before, I’d thought of the “Heo Un” employees in the pop-up store.

Especially Mr. Jang Heo Un, who died.

...When I summoned him into Se-gwang Special City’s subway station, he suddenly came to his senses.

As if the contaminated, urban-legend version of him—only the resort-employee persona left—had never existed.

And this station is Ohu Station, where that pop-up store is.

The location where the “Pleasant Theme Park” space I leased exists.

Maybe that’s why it might work again—I tried raising Deputy Eun Haje as my “employee”....

And it really worked.

If it hadn’t worked properly and only the “resort employee” persona had revived, I was going to erase the line immediately.

Thankfully, it didn’t seem necessary.

Thank god....

But can I even call this thank god?

How is this possible? I don’t know the cause.

And is it right to... bring him back like this?

Should I have let him die here so I could save him later?

I don’t know.

No.

Living comes first.

Being able to talk like this matters!

Swallowing the anxiety and impatience surging up in my throat, I kept my face as composed as I could and stopped Deputy Eun Haje from moving too much.

“You can’t sit up too violently. It could cause serious problems with your wounds.”

“Wounds? Hm....”

Looking down at his abdomen, Deputy Eun Haje’s expression turned strange.

Because the traces of that horrific blood loss were still there, untouched.

To pull his gaze away, I changed the subject.

“I treated you and stopped the bleeding, and you shouldn’t be in danger. But the situation is urgent, so we need to move. You have to get out of this city—”

“Hm?”

Deputy Eun Haje laughed.

“No. Why would I follow you?”

“...!”

“And where’d our guys run off to, and why were two idiots and a kid standing over me? I’m curious.”

Before I could even make an excuse, the next sharp question landed right under my chin.

“And did we know each other?”

“...You probably don’t recognize me.”

“Then it’s one-sided. Your face is clearly the face of someone looking at a person you know.”

“...!”

“Haha. So it is. Well, so you were just staring at a collapsed person you know....”

He propped his chin on his hand.

“So why should I travel with someone I don’t know?”

“.......”

“Sure, I can see you’re not malicious just from your face, but... it’s a terror situation, and I got hit by a car.”

He flicked his hand.

“You don’t travel with suspicious people. Anyway, good luck.”

I realized it.

...He won’t be easy to persuade.

If I say I’m an agent, he’ll probably scoff.

This “reporter” was oddly more openly sharp than the deputy I knew—his confidence and self-certainty flashing.

I looked into two eyes that cut through even this noisy, desperate concourse.

Then—

“...What if I get you out of this madhouse?”

“Well.”

“What if I give you a scoop?”

His eyes changed.

Now it felt like we could finally speak.

“What kind of scoop?”

“It’s related to the terror.”

Quietly, I said,

“Reporter. Starting now, I’m going to uncover the cause and identity behind the terror.”

Reporter Eun Haje’s eyes gleamed, and he held out his hand.

“Deal.”

“.......”

“So you’re in the same line of work. Nice to meet you.”

I shook his hand.

...He doesn’t really believe me.

It’s purely an observer’s gaze.

Still, at least I got his permission to travel together.

Then, beside me, Ryu Jaegwan whispered.

“Um, agent. Then are we taking the subway to Se-gwang Station?”

But Reporter Eun Haje cut in.

“To Se-gwang Station? The subway’s still running?”

Well.

“It was running in only one direction.”

“...That’s weird. If it’s terror, trains usually switch to non-stop pass-through or stop service entirely. Didn’t control contact you?”

“There were station staff on that platform.”

“Hm. So they haven’t completely let go.”

Leaning to look around, Reporter Eun Haje grinned and rose fully to his feet.

“Either way, running it in some weird way means there’s something going on. Let’s go.”

“...!”

“You said Se-gwang Station. Didn’t you?”

I hesitated briefly.

...We’d already lost a lot of time. Now even moving outside the subway wasn’t something I could call safe.

I had to recalculate.

Then.

Moving inside the subway first, then leaving when a wave of the crowd passes, wouldn’t be a terrible idea.

The subway is relatively safe.

And if we go to Se-gwang Station and gather information, there aren’t many people as capable as Deputy Eun Haje in that direction.

“...All right. We’ll move to Se-gwang Station together.”

“Okay.”

Watching my former boss speak so briskly, I gave a faint smile.

Even now, he still felt reliable.

And so we boarded the subway one step late and headed for Se-gwang Station.

To track the Counseling Teacher’s traces.

Chapter 340

We managed to get on a train heading for Se-gwang Station about twenty minutes later.

[My goodness, at this rate we’ll get trampled to death! What a filthy spectacle!]

We had to let several trains go by.

I almost got crushed trying to board.

Because this was the point when people who’d suffered serious injuries while running started refusing—desperately—to get off the train.

They realized it. That as long as they’re on the train, their bodies stay intact.

It was a madhouse of people trying to get on and people shoving them back.

...In fact, I saw someone get crushed to death.

...Hoo.

Watching the horrific scene splatter itself across everything without giving me a second to intervene, I ground my teeth and kept the other two close.

“This way.”

And without forcing it, we moved to the very end of the platform—where there were relatively fewer people—and tried to board calmly.

The doors are opening.

As expected.

People in a panic were surging to board along the same route like some kind of lemming effect, and the last car—away from that flow—was relatively rational.

...For now.

I shoved the two of them in and managed to cluster right in front of the door.

The screen doors are closing....

Good.

Hoo.

My insides felt so twisted up with relief and tension that it bordered on pain.

But Reporter Eun Haje looked at me and lifted one eyebrow.

“You’re pretty capable. You’re calm.”

“Thank you.”

“It’s chaos outside, but people in here aren’t in terrible shape, at least. From what I heard on the way, it sounded like a zombie virus outbreak.”

I answered evenly.

“The underground is relatively safe.”

“Is it? Weird. If it’s bio-chemical terror, toxic stuff settles. Normally the underground should be more dangerous.”

Sharp.

“And... how does that stiff over there know that kind of thing?”

“.......”

I checked our surroundings.

Even with all this noise, nobody seemed to have the spare focus to eavesdrop. Everyone was drowned in their own pain and desperation—trying to contact someone, trembling with acquaintances, staring outside.

I opened my mouth.

“...Please think of me as someone involved who’s used to situations like this.”

“Does that have something to do with being called an agent?”

“.......”

So he did hear it.

“Yes.”

I gave Reporter Eun Haje a brief explanation about the Disaster Management Bureau. As he listened, his eyes flashed.

Even if it sounds ridiculous, it’s an explanation that fits the situation.

And when I finished saying, “There are people I have to rescue and get out, and it’s already in progress,” he laughed broadly and said,

“Right. ‘Agent,’ then you’ve got to write a report too, don’t you?”

“Maybe.”

“Then you’ll need someone to record what you did. Right?”

Then he pointed at himself and grinned.

“I’m perfect for that.”

“.......”

“Not many people are better than me at tracking conspiracies and getting a handle on incidents. Objectively speaking, I’ll write it up with solid analysis and interpretation.”

No matter how I looked at it, he seemed completely full of the desire to publish.

Fine... whatever.

Holding back a sigh, I nodded.

“I’d appreciate that.”

“Okay. Good choice.”

Reporter Eun Haje gave me a wink.

But this man wouldn’t be able to write that article as a reporter anyway.

What was here wasn’t a real reporter, but Deputy Eun Haje’s contaminated form—fixed into a shape that suited Se-gwang Special City’s disaster day.

...In fact, that was why I’d also tried using the resort contract—using an “employer’s order”—to force him back to his original state.

It didn’t work.

All I’d done was add a single line of resort transfer to the existing contract, and beyond that, I couldn’t exert any power at all.

If I could have, he would’ve changed into resort workwear and even his injuries would’ve healed—so this meant it didn’t work at all.

My ability as a resort manager.

So in Se-gwang Special City, is the influence of other urban legends blocked?

Up to now I’d thought it was because everything was sealed off, but maybe it was the nature of the disaster itself—starting from City Hall....

.......

Wait.

The train shakes.

“Huh?”

The car is tilting.

“Huh? Huhhh?!”

“Wh—?”

“Oh my god!”

People grabbed the straps, but those who couldn’t—or who slipped—slid as they were, pouring to one side.

The opposite side from where we were standing.

To the right.

“Aaaah!”

My blood went cold.

With my arm hooked around a pole, I reached out to grab the other two. Reporter Eun Haje, already holding another pole, and Ryu Jaegwan, the student holding the same pole as me, crossed hands and clutched my arm and shoulder.

We were practically floating as we dangled crookedly against the left-side doors.

“What is this? What is this?!”

“H-hhk!”

The train was still moving, but it was terrifyingly unstable.

“Agent, there’s a strange sound....”

It wasn’t the friction noise of a curve.

It was....

Something breaking.

Ah.

A train running with more passengers than its load could handle.

And then, the train that couldn’t bear the weight anymore—derailing—

was overturning.

KIIIIIIIIIING—!

“Gaaah!”

“Save me!”

Screams came from the front car, and without losing speed, the train kept running as it rocked harder and harder—

then began to topple heavily, completely.

“Aaaah!”

The pole we were holding rose toward the ceiling. People collapsing in a rush piled up below like spilled cargo, bones snapping as they stacked.

Anyone pinned at the very bottom would already be pulp—crushed to death.

Buried.

The train was still moving, the body bouncing.

No!

If I let go like this, I’d get swept away.

Calm down, calm down... fuck!

I clenched my teeth and prepared to land as carefully as possible.

Before it fully flips, I have to slide down to the floor.

I just had to slide down the pole.

The two people’s hands beside me were slick with tension and cold sweat. I sent them down first, then drew a breath and pulled the arm holding the pole—so I could slide down last.

And I saw it.

Outside the window, blue light is rippling.

“......!”

A goblin.

For a split second, I almost blanked out—then snapped back and twisted to look at the device attached beside the door.

Emergency release...!

I operated the manual mechanism, putting the door into a state where it could be opened.

Hurry!

And then, teeth bared, I climbed the pole again and reached for the door to open it—

“Agent, step on me...!”

“...!”

Using high schooler Ryu Jaegwan’s shoulder—his back offered as a foothold—I launched myself more easily and grabbed the door.

Then I cracked it open.

Whoosh.

A hazy light slipped through the gap.

The blue trace of the goblinfire hovered around the door.

To anyone watching, it would look like rescue....

...A trace?

I couldn’t see any expression or personality in the goblinfire.

A lump of energy that had lost its purpose.

Then...!

I released the hand hanging from the pole.

“...!”

Hanging on with one hand from the door mounted to the ceiling, I slipped my other hand through the gap—

and snatched the goblinfire’s trace.

Then I hurled it hard beneath the train.

Huk.

The sacred flame of a cooperative spirit—recognizing the status of my body as an agent—ran along my hand, connected down under the subway wheels.

Blue light flashed, and in the next instant—

With a grinding crackle, the train flips back again.

“...!!”

“Uegh...!”

Not too abruptly, the train rotated ninety degrees back the other way. The door I was holding shut on its own. I heard the wheels spinning uselessly—

click.

The sound of wheels locking back onto rails.

“.......”

The train is departing....

The scenery beyond the windows began to move smoothly again....

“...Ha.”

Gripping the door, I let out a long breath.

“Are you alright?”

“Haa... more or less?”

“Y-yes!”

My party was safe. With relief, I could hear my own heartbeat pounding inside my head.

“Seriously... that stiff guy is flying around.”

At Reporter Eun Haje’s admiration, I gave a weak smile.

But inside the car was already hell.

“Hhng....”

“Minjeong? Minjeong!”

The sounds of corpses crushed to death, and the wailing of people with shattered bones, filled the air.

Right next to us—close enough our bodies could collide.

In a car packed with people, now there were bodies too.

Damn it.

Reflexively, I pulled the high schooler away from there and shoved him even closer to the door.

Reporter Eun Haje, on the other hand, was watching the carnage with a surprising amount of focus.

My hair stood on end, but I still forced myself up on trembling legs and looked at the mass of people.

It was too horrific... but maybe because I’d seen too many deaths all day, I felt a strangely desolate, dry exhaustion.

Staggering, I started pulling survivors out of the pile. And when the inside of the car was more or less cleared up, I withdrew my hands—

“Wow, thank you. Agent I don’t know. If you weren’t here, I’d have died.”

...!!

I whipped my head around.

Over there, beyond the crowd, in the front car—several new figures had appeared inside the train before I even noticed.

Figures gathering the pinned bodies and the injured.

People wearing blue jackets shaped almost exactly like mine.

Rope restraints, glass hand cannons,

...blue goblinfire traces.

And.

“If this hadn’t been cleaned up before the next train came, it would’ve been a real disaster.”

A person wearing a traditional lion mask had appeared right beside me, chattering away.

Under the mask, on the blue jacket, I could see the letters.

Supernatural Disaster Management Bureau.

Disaster Management Bureau agents.

...!!

Se-gwang Special City’s disaster day.

The agents who’d been dispatched were standing right in front of my eyes.

Among them, the lion-mask agent looked at me—the one who’d helped reconnect the train to the rails.

Then, in a voice that sounded cheerful but faintly anxious, he asked,

“Wow, you even cleaned up this car in the meantime. You handled the goblinfire really well earlier—what branch are you from, Agent?”

.......

“I’m not from the Se-gwang City branch.”

“Hoo, got it.”

The eyes inside the playful, rough lion mask seemed to glint yellow as he looked at my jacket.

“...But that outfit—doesn’t look like regulation. Looks like a fake.”

“That’s right.”

I answered on purpose, a little awkwardly.

“I’m on break, so I didn’t have clothes. I borrowed a uniform in a hurry from a local cooperative spirit around here.”

“Ha. Yeah. ‘If you don’t have teeth, you chew with your gums’—that’s exactly something a field agent would say....”

“Ha ha.”

He laughed loudly along with my bitter smile.

I quickly scanned his condition....

...Male? Female?

I couldn’t even tell.

Like a Daydream mask, the presence clouded perception—making it hard to grasp appearance or traits.

I couldn’t tell whether the voice was high or low, either. The lion mask’s playful impression felt like it pressed all individuality flat.

...Or is it the goblin’s power?

Sure enough, in a slightly troubled voice, he brought up goblins.

“Agent, do you also have a goblinfire that travels with you? My partner goblinfire suddenly evaporated this morning. Just vanished—left only an afterimage.”

Of course it did.

The real goblinfires would’ve all been pulled out before the sealing ritual was carried out.

They wouldn’t exist inside Se-gwang Special City’s May 4—trapped in disaster day.

But I didn’t show it.

“...I’m on break, so I don’t have goblinfire or equipment. But if what you’re saying is true, this isn’t normal.”

“Mm.... Yeah. Normally that guy sticks to me like a rice cake.”

Scratching his mask, the lion-mask agent said,

“What’s weird is, there are still plenty of traces. Like... the goblinfire’s self is gone, but some auxiliary-battery kind of thing is still left behind.”

“.......”

That was—

Could it be that the goblinfire power used on Se-gwang Special City’s disaster day is still trapped in here, lingering?

Like what happened at Se-gwang Technical High School.

“Did you hear anything else when you borrowed that uniform from the cooperative spirit?”

“Nothing in particular.”

Instead, I relayed what the “Counseling Teacher” had said—that he would go to Se-gwang Station to end this disaster.

“I see. Mm....”

The lion-mask agent turned his head.

From the front car, I watched agents more or less calming people down and separating the bodies, and swallowed.

And my party....

“So you’re both in the same Disaster Management Bureau, but you don’t know each other? Sounds like departments are pretty split up, and there are separate branches by region?”

“Aha ha. Yep. That’s right.”

The lion-mask agent breezily waved off Reporter Eun Haje’s words, then suddenly ruffled only high schooler Ryu Jaegwan’s hair and said with a smile,

“Looks like you’re the rescue requesters? Are you Blue Dragon Team?”

I gave a bitter smile.

And before I knew it, I answered.

“Actually, Black Turtle Team. But I don’t have equipment, so... I’m just doing what I can to rescue people.”

“Wha! That’s awesome. Fellow agent.”

Then he dug through his pocket and pulled something out.

A shining jade-colored firearm made of glass.

A glass hand cannon.

“I’ll give you this. I brought a spare.”

“...Thank you.”

“No. I’m the one who should thank you—I lived because of you. Seriously, I almost died trying to stand the train back up with only a goblinfire afterimage.”

I quickly tucked the glass hand cannon into my chest. Watching, the lion-mask agent nodded and said,

“Then you’ll be getting off at Se-gwang Station. You’ll check the cooperative spirit’s condition and re-map the rescue route.”

“Yes.”

“Then....”

The lion-mask agent hesitated a little and took in the train’s carnage and the citizens’ condition.

During our conversation, he’d seemed cheerful the entire time, but now, underneath that expression, anxiety and unease flickered into view—then vanished.

And so my party wouldn’t hear, he lowered his voice and whispered in my ear.

“Hey, I’ve got a bad feeling. I’m catching the smell of that vicious tin-can bastard in here.”

“Tin-can... bastard?”

“I mean the Daydream Inc. director. That fraud!”

......!

Which director was he talking about?

And tin-can... bastard.

In other words, “steel bastard.”

A wicked fire-breathing imugi monster... wasn’t it?

[Hm, a folktale that was popular in my friend’s hometown! Should I call it classic, or just old? Of course, if he’s been a famous figure since the seventeenth century, we could treat it as a classic.]

Right. But there’s a director like that at Daydream Inc.?

No—more importantly.

“You can smell... that?”

“I’m not wearing this hot, heavy mask for no reason. I wear it for its sacred effects.”

“...!”

The lion mask rippled as he rolled those yellow eyes playfully.

“Especially this guy—he sniffs out evil, strong bastards and unfair contracts like nothing else! He’s basically a bloodhound, right?”

O-oh. I see.

But before that, another realization hit.

Pleasant Research Institute was ruined because of Daydream Inc.

If so, then there’s a high chance Daydream Inc. is also involved in the Se-gwang Special City incident tied to Pleasant Research Institute...!

Maybe even right now.

I asked urgently.

“Do you think Daydream Inc. is deeply connected to all this?”

“Probably, yeah. And there’s something else that bothers me.”

What?

At the lion-mask agent’s gesture, I moved closer and put my ear near him.

In an even lower, smaller voice, he said—

“That smell is coming off you, too.”

I froze.

“Agent. Did you sign some kind of contract with a Daydream Inc. director? Maybe....”

“.......”

“Maybe you’re impersonating an agent, stirring up trouble?”

Chapter 341

I swallowed.

The lion mask’s sacred, terrifying face was staring at me.

Those two yellow, jagged eyes moved inside the mask as if they were alive on their own.

“Did you know? Lion masks have all sorts of traditions depending on the region... and one of them is driving off evil spirits.”

“.......”

The lion mask’s mane poured over my shoulder.

It looked like the mask could open a snout full of sharp teeth at any moment.

“And isn’t harming citizens because you’re bewitched by that tin-can bastard an evil spirit too~?”

I watched that chilling thing draw closer.

Watched it....

“...No.”

And answered.

“I have no intention of impersonating an agent and stirring up trouble during a mass disaster like this.”

“Oh?”

“And you already know I’m innocent, Agent.”

“Why do you think that?”

The lion mask’s head tilted, its mane sliding down my back.

It felt cold. Wrong.

A bead of sweat ran down the back of my neck.

I need to sort this out properly.

Forcing down my rising panic, I spoke evenly.

“You wouldn’t have lent me agent equipment if you thought I was guilty.”

“...!”

“If I looked suspicious, you wouldn’t give me gear that helps someone escape. Isn’t that right?”

I barely turned my eyes to check my party.

Maybe they assumed we were having “agent talk,” because the two of them were watching the surrounding carnage instead of us, without much suspicion.

“If you really can distinguish evil by smell, then you should know I don’t have malice or guilt.”

Maybe he’d judged me sincere by watching the people I was trying to rescue—and how I treated them.

Please.

Trying to loosen the tension that felt like it was curling my fingers into claws, I continued.

“It’s true that I signed a contract with a Daydream Inc. director. But that has nothing to do with what I’m doing from this point on.”

“.......”

The lion mask swayed, moving as if it were peering into me.

“What are you going to do?”

“As I said, I’m going to rescue those two people. There are people I have to save. And....”

After a moment of conflict, I said it.

“If possible, I want to end this disaster.”

The lion mask fell silent.

Then the mask shook, and—

“You mean it!”

The mane whipped back.

I sucked in a breath.

In the same instant, the lion mask—back to its playful movements—grabbed my arm.

“Then I’m counting on you for the rescue, fellow agent!”

“...Yes.”

Hoo.

Watching the lion-mask agent return to being a friendly, affable agent, I let out a breath of relief only inside my chest.

But tension still coiled through my body.

...The lion mask still wasn’t letting go of my arm.

...Strangely, it feels like I’m standing in front of something out of an urban legend.

Because of that lion mask? I could barely feel any humanity from him at all.

The lion-mask agent twisted his head in a warm, familiar way.

But he didn’t move away from me, and he absolutely wouldn’t release my arm.

As if he’d drag me off the second I made a wrong move.

“So how did you end up letting that vicious tin-can bastard coil up inside you?”

“.......”

For the record, I didn’t even know who Mr. Tin-Can was.

One of Daydream Inc.’s directors, I guess.

But I can’t say something like this here—

—I signed contracts with two Development Division directors, and one of them is a local cooperative spirit who lost it after taking a bite and went insane. Oh, and before that I also signed an employment contract, so maybe I’m contracted to some other HR-side director too. Like octopus legs, you know?

...I couldn’t say that. I swallowed it and summarized broadly instead.

“You mean how I ended up contracting with Daydream Inc.?”

“That’s right!”

Then there was a simple, clear answer.

“I was tricked.”

“Ah.”

“And I’m looking for a way out.”

The lion-mask agent looked like he was sinking into thought. Then the lion mask’s white mane bobbed up and down.

“Mm. So you’re on leave so you don’t burden the Bureau?”

“Not only that... honestly, it’s for my own safety too.”

Because I was a spy.

But I wasn’t now, so it felt unfair....

“Sure, sure!”

Thankfully, he went along with it smoothly. Hoo.

Letting out a breath, I looked at the remaining stops.

...About eight minutes left.

So we’re almost at Se-gwang Station.

If I got detained by agents at a time like this and got delayed any further, I might not be able to find the Counseling Teacher at Se-gwang Station.

Then I’d lose everything.

I need to defuse this fast....

But then a suddenly colder voice brushed my ear.

“That’s strange too.”

“...Pardon?”

“Then how did a fellow agent who’s on leave even find out about this disaster and come in to rescue someone in this city?”

“...!”

“It hasn’t even been half a day—no, not even a few hours—since the disaster broke out. And yet you happened to find citizens to rescue and ended up here!”

“.......”

“Even if news got out, it’s too short. It’s awfully convenient!”

It was a sharp question.

And it was exactly right.

The timeline doesn’t match.

From the instant the disaster alert arrived, I’d already been inside this city.

And agents from other cities only begin entering Se-gwang Special City after a good amount of time passes.

So “I entered because there was a rescue request” wouldn’t work. Time-wise, it didn’t add up.

...I needed time to think of an answer. I didn’t have any. Damn it!

I need to turn it back on him....

As I racked my brain, I noticed something.

The agents in the front car, over there.

...!

“...If we’re going to talk like that, you’re suspicious too.”

“Huh? Me?”

“Yes.”

I spoke evenly.

“You aren’t from the Se-gwang City branch either, are you?”

“...cWhat makes you think that?”

“Look over there.”

Following my gaze, the lion-mask agent’s eyes went to—

the agents in the front car, handling the accident and explaining “supernatural disaster” to the citizens.

“The agents over there—and the lion-mask agent right in front of me—your jacket details are different.”

“.......”

On the front car agents’ jacket pockets, there were patches with the Se-gwang City logo.

The lion-mask agent checked his own jacket pocket—clean, with nothing—and lifted his head.

"I think so, too. How can anyone be this irresponsible."

"......"

"I definitely received that kind of promise from them."

The air twisted strangely.

"That on the day their efforts succeeded, this city would become safer for people than anywhere else on the surface."

Lee Heo Un had no answer.

No—he couldn’t answer.

I saw the researcher twisting his own hands around his throat.

...!

In this fantastical space packed with colorful toys, he rolled on the floor with choking sounds.

But the Counseling Teacher smiled at me and explained, as if kindly going over pre-counseling notes.

"I’ve lived for a very long time in Se-gwang Special City—this region that in the past was called Gwangjin. At one time, I harmed people this way and that, but after I grew attached and gained reason, I kept looking after and protecting them. So those words sounded very pleasing."

His calm, rational way of speaking gradually turned softer and gentler.

As if he were returning to his original self.

"Because the government men who followed those people also guaranteed it, I yielded the underground of this land and listened to what they asked. And then, one day, I heard an earnest request."

"...What request was that?"

"A request asking whether they could borrow a wondrous orb for just a little while."

At the Counseling Teacher’s—no, the sacred being’s—gesture, the image of a bright, round sphere floated in midair above his fingertip.

Its many-colored brilliance glowed red.

I remembered how Deputy Eun Haje had used a similar orb on some previous day to let an old man infiltrate the Brilliant Anonymous Faith....

But that one had been finger-sized, and it hadn’t looked nearly as numinous.

"Have you ever heard the folk tradition about fox marbles? Not the berries—the orb a fox monster possesses."

It glittered.

"Some say it comes from a dragon’s wish orb, but it probably became a saying because similar stories were passed down.... The story that it grants wishes."

"...!"

Soon, that fantastic shape vanished.

But I noticed it reminded me of the egg-shaped form that had been sitting in the middle of the dream incubator’s tank....

...A wish.

No way.

"Are you saying what you lent them was used in this experiment?"

"That seems to be the case, doesn't it?"

A laughing sound rang out.

"Without knowing that, I hurried to retrieve my orb and take action. Protecting my domain and pushing out other unclean things was something the Bureau had repeatedly asked of me with great care, holding rites for me as well. But...."

His voice turned severe.

"They say the orb has disappeared."

"......"

"So now there’s no way. There is no way to protect this city from the unclean infiltration outside, and from the infection that spreads like a plague."

The researcher’s face turned bluish. His hands trembled thinly, as if he might lose consciousness at any moment.

Sending Lee Heo Un off like this is meaningless.

Besides, it wasn’t this person’s responsibility alone, and we’d lose information.

In the end, I spoke quickly.

"Can’t you track how the orb disappeared?"

"......"

......

......

"That's true."

"Kh—!"

Lee Heo Un flung both hands off his own throat as if jolted by electricity.

But at that moment.

Researcher Lee Heo Un’s right ear plopped onto the floor.

"...!"

What had fallen was now, unmistakably, a plastic toy....

"......"

The dazzling corridor visible past Lee Jaheon and the other two somehow felt quieter than before.

Among the colorful toys, several had stopped moving and stood still, or seemed to have lost their liveliness....

As if they had lost their mystery.

"...Run quickly. Th-this office won’t hold out much longer. You have to get out...."

Researcher Lee Heo Un blurted out urgently.

"Right now, we’re protected from the fire and the disaster outside, but the flames will break in here soon. There isn’t much time left!"

Wait.

I remembered.

The moment the Counseling Teacher’s hand that had been shoved into the monster was severed—how it went limp and vanished, as if it disappeared.

...If that was the case.

No way.

"......"

"That's right."

...!!

There was even a faint thrill in the researcher’s voice.

"Someone... came."

My head went cold with an emotion I couldn’t tell was anger or fear.

But my reason caught the wrongness.

"That’s strange. If anomalous phenomena disappeared, it should be safer. Then why is it like that outside?"

-This isn't what we research. Someone tampered with it!

Everyone’s gaze turned toward the door.

Reporter Eun Haje was scratching his head.

"I'll be honest, the content of this conversation makes me feel like, what the hell is this even about—but the context is definitely my specialty. Usually that's how the world works. Who benefits the most from things turning into this?"

The researcher’s eyes sank.

It was true, but it looked like it was hard for him to make that kind of guess by temperament.

In that case.

Then I’ll just make it more specific.

"Well said, citizen."

[Excellent interrogation. Friend!]

The researcher, fumbling, got up and rummaged through what looked like his desk.

A group photo of several people had the heading "Corporate Cooperation."

Director Cheong.

Horror and rejection flashed across Lee Heo Un’s face, then quickly turned into resignation and bitterness.

The next moment, Researcher Lee Heo Un’s thumb fell to the floor.

It was plastic as well.

The Counseling Teacher didn’t answer.

Lee Heo Un couldn’t lift his head, and the movements of the dazzling toys in the corridor and office began to stop intermittently.

Lee Jaheon stared at him as if he felt no emotion at the thoughtful words.

...A button item?

I couldn’t see Lee Jaheon’s face, but I could see him stepping out into the corridor instead of nodding.

"I will guide you to the subway entrance."

At that, Researcher Lee Heo Un seemed reassured.

"I'll look for another way, so it would be best if everyone left."

Starting with Reporter Eun Haje, everyone filed out of the office door.

And when even the Counseling Teacher had gone out into the corridor for the moment....

Beyond his regret and despair, a researcher’s sense of achievement flickered briefly across Lee Heo Un’s face.

"About that, I am."

"I see."

The puzzled look in Lee Heo Un’s eyes changed the moment his gaze met mine.

Lee Heo Un’s eyes hurriedly scanned me.

As if he’d realized it wasn’t a formal agent uniform, but the outfit of a cooperative sacred being—the Counseling Teacher.

Biiiriririririck!!!

The fire alarm began blaring in the corridor.

Let’s go.

It was the moment a chill beyond unease crept over me.

A soft voice spoke.

Damn it.

"Agent."

No way.

And for no reason at all, I knew.

"D Team."

If I could see his face, that lizard’s snout would have been curving into a smile right now.

Chapter 344

"? I always remembered."

Because he’d fallen into this place under conditions that weren’t all that different from mine.

We both got out of the loop and then... well, we died.

He knows I lied.

Please don’t make subject-inference remarks in front of the Counseling Teacher that make the entire meaning obvious.

If we succeeded in retrieving the orb, maybe we really could stop this disaster day and bring the urban legend to an end.

The people who had evacuated into Se-gwang Station stayed alive at least until they fell into despair that no rescue was coming.

We have to go and come back before this corridor is swallowed by the fire.

Aaaagh.

Right. Since this was the day the incident broke out, the situation could be different.

To the emergency stairs.

[1F]

Chapter 345

The moment the rooftop access door opened, stabbing sunlight poured into my eyes.

And the instant my vision adjusted, I saw the Se-gwang City Hall rooftop beneath a chillingly clear blue sky.

...The floor was covered in a strange grayish-white substance, like a membrane.

Like the shell of a cocoon.

But it shimmered with iridescent colors.

...An egg.

I remembered the footage Reporter Eun Haje’s group had taken.

That bizarre shape, swelling atop the very top of city hall.

It looked like it had “swollen” all the way down onto the rooftop floor.

"......"

Straining not to let my hands shake, I slid my hand into my pocket and carefully pulled out a bundle of convenience store receipts.

Then I threw them.

Tap.

The receipts rolled across the cocoon-like surface of the rooftop floor, bumped into the railing, and fell over the edge.

And naturally, the city spread out beyond that....

"Don’t look."

"......"

Forcing down the tension that bristled up my spine, I turned my head back toward the rooftop.

...In the center of the rooftop, all by itself, there was a single machine.

The dream incubator.

Around the base of that massive machine, toy fragments and a researcher’s lab coat, glasses, and clothes were strewn like trash.

And in front of it, something stood upright.

A silhouette about the size of a person.

Covered in cloth.

"......"

I stepped forward carefully.

I felt a strangely soft give underfoot, and....

I walked forward.

[Bold, aren’t you, friend!]

I reached out... and yanked the cloth off the silhouette.

I met eyes drawn in a retro cartoon style.

It wasn’t a person.

A Daydream Inc. character.

It was a life-size standee panel of a yellow dragon character.

That flat panel, printed with a retro comic-style yellow dragon, stood grotesquely beside the dream incubator, and in the blank space next to it, a single blue button was attached with an explanation.

CLICK

-> A congratulatory message from Yongyong has been recorded.

(You’ll get different content every time you press it!)

"......"

A sense of wrongness swelled into dread.

"Should I press it?"

"...No."

Swallowing, I raised my hand and pressed the button myself.

A cheerful voice spilled out through a low-quality recording.

"Hello, Pleasant Research Institute researchers!

I’m a commemorative panel produced by Director Cheongdallae of Daydream Inc. to celebrate the Pleasant Research Institute’s experiment this time.

It’s a great honor for me, Yongyong, to be present at the key experiment site for achieving your research goal.

You must be waiting for the results, so I hope I can help ease your boredom for a moment!

...But to be honest, I also came for company promotion. Daydream Inc. is launching a new product line, so please support us!"

That childlike voice, distorted and crackling, echoed across the empty rooftop.

If a researcher had pressed it while waiting for the results, they would’ve burst out laughing....

Now, only this panel remained, standing alone.

"......"

I pressed the button again.

"Daydream Inc. and the Pleasant Research Institute have been in an active cooperative relationship from the start.

Because Director Cheong was deeply moved by the Pleasant Research Institute’s meaningful research goal.

‘A safe world without anomalous phenomena’! What an amazing catchphrase.

Daydream Inc. has respected the Pleasant Research Institute’s catchphrase and cooperated up to now.

It might change direction a little, though!

So, for today’s memorable and decisive experiment, Director Cheong prepared a surprise gift for a very long time....

A gift that will reduce your workload.

Curious?

Look at the dream incubator."

My head turned reflexively. My eyes began raking over the rooftop dream incubator.

As a commemoration.

In just five minutes, your grand plan to cultivate a resident inside the tank will end in an unfortunate accident—oh? You’re going to open the incubator?

Oh dear. Yesterday I changed the screw-lock system. It’s in a state where you can’t open it with the method you normally use.

If you don’t plan to give up, still... hmm, maybe you could force it open?"

The incubator was covered in countless desperate traces.

Traces so blatant and chilling, like they’d struggled to tear it open until the very end.

Huh?

-‘Angel’s Sigh’ tape recording. ‘Listen to heaven’s auspicious melody at a reasonable price.’

From a slot machine prize at Body Casino Station.

Was it... that melody...?

This melody parasitizes people and gives them vitality and regenerative power, but if you overuse it, the parasite eventually eats the person. Because it wasn’t a melody meant for humans.

And it spreads in every direction—mentally and physically!

A perfect function for making a world, right?"

I clenched my fist tight.

Summoning just one ‘resident of a place without anomalous phenomena.’

“Something to be grateful for. I accomplished your slogan for you.”

Insane.

This is insane....

Creak.

It’s fine.

Brown.

Because I kept eating the berries the Counseling Teacher—Hoyuwon—gave me.

......!!

What is this?

"Agent!"

"Cover your head with it. Make sure the wet cloth covers your airway! I won’t look back."

"......"

Thankfully, I heard the sound of cloth being pulled into place. Letting out a breath of relief, I hoisted him onto my back.

The strangely weightless feeling was gone, but he was still light for an adult man.

As if there were places missing.

"...I’m opening it."

Heat blasted into my nose.

"......"

Flames were burning in places all over the Pleasant Research Institute core floor.

But I could see the route.

I can make it.

With my water-soaked sleeve covering {N•o•v•e•l•i•g•h•t} my mouth, I sprinted inside before the smoke thickened.

Huff—short breaths sounded from above my shoulder and from my own mouth. As fast as I could—careful of the ceiling and walls, especially—I ran, avoiding smoke.

I can do it.

We can get out.

The office, stripped of its mystery, wasn’t that long. I ran the distance.

Four meters,

Three meters,

Two meters,

One meter....

My hand grabbed the doorknob.

I yanked it open hard.

Whoosh—the musty air of the subway hit my nose. Corpse stench or not, the important thing was we’d escaped the inferno together....

Chapter 346

I lay pinned beneath collapsed concrete, listening to the voices outside.

I tried to move my mouth, but no sound came out.

Only a hazy, dull drift of voices seeped into my head....

The voices of Se-gwang City’s Disaster Management Bureau agents, thinks thick with deep fatigue and bewilderment.

“First, we’ve prevented any further collapse. ...Over the radio, we’re switching from closure to rescue-focused operations from here on....”

“There are citizens in the concourse too.... ...Like this, too many people have died....”

“What do we do, Team Leader? The selection criteria....”

The voices grew more and more distant....

‘Ah.’

They won’t have time to save the person pinned down here. I understood the situation, very slowly.

The sound of their boots moving away—leaving the collapsed pile of concrete behind—spread as vibrations along the cold rebar pressed to my cheek....

“Agents.”

At that moment, a familiar voice rang out.

The Counseling Teacher’s voice.

Hoarse and cracked, it sounded low and clogged, as though they too were trapped inside the concrete like me....

“There is an agent, in here.......”

“...!!”

“They’re down, there.”

A brief stir seemed to rise among the agents, and then they drew closer.

“Who are you?”

“I... I’m the counselor at Se-gwang High School.”

“Ah!”

As if they recognized the identity at once, hurried footsteps rushed in. Then came the noise of something like concrete and rebar debris being cleared away.

With that movement, a structural fragment embedded in my back collapsed further to the side. I registered the pain spreading through my body in a dull thud....

I couldn’t move.

“Wait.”

A calm voice.

“...How did someone like you end up pinned down under there?”

“Pardon...? I’m... just a counselor....”

“.......”

A heavy silence fell. Through it, the sound of clearing debris continued....

“Something’s off. Back up.”

“Just a moment. If we move this... ngh!”

“...!!”

“H-hey, wait.”

At last, the sound of a heavy piece of debris dropping—someone sucking in a breath.

As if they’d seen something they never expected.

“The way they look....”

“It’s contamination. Get back.”

Footsteps, equipment clacking, friction scraping through the air....

“No, cough, it’s... not contamination.”

The Counseling Teacher’s voice, as though exposed to the open air now, was no longer muffled.

“Don’t worry....”

But it sank weakly, breathy.

“...‘Counselor.’ Are you aware of what state you’re in right now?”

“Yes. I’m... fine. But, down there....”

They sounded desperate.

“There’s an agent who carried me out on their back....”

“.......”

“They have special... abilities. They’ll definitely be useful.... So,”

Silence flowed.

None of the agents answered.

“...Agents?”

“What is that agent’s agent-name?”

“I... I still haven’t heard it.”

“...Then what about their affiliation? You should be able to tell from their jacket.”

“.......”

Slowly, an answer came.

“The jacket is... here. They gave it to me.... They aren’t... wearing it.”

“.......”

“.......”

“Isn’t this your jacket?”

“It looks... that way. But in truth, it isn’t....”

“.......”

“.......”

“Understood.”

“Agent...?”

I heard them backing away.

Their low conversation between themselves carried to my ears through the collapsed concrete....

“We can’t spend any more time. ...Move.”

“But...!”

“The circumstances don’t add up. That sacred being is already not normal. Even if we rescue them, we won’t be able to get much help. It’s already....”

“......B-but even if they aren’t an agent, there’s a person under there... they’ll die soon...!”

“Agent!”

The sound of someone grabbing a shoulder.

“Right now, there are tens of thousands of people in this city who are dying.”

“.......”

“And you want to spend more time for just one person whose survival isn’t even certain? If we make an exception to the rules, what happens to the other citizens we couldn’t save?”

“...Th-”

“Go. Before other citizens get here!”

But then.

“That line....”

The Counseling Teacher’s voice, slow.

“It’s too familiar.”

“.......”

“I’ve... heard it before, I think.”

I swallowed.

“What?”

“Don’t answer.”

“So, yesterday...?”

.......

“No... the day before yesterday too, and before that, and before that, and before that before that, and before that before that before that before that.......”

“H-hey, wait.”

“Back up!”

Even in the commotion outside, even through my already fogged mind, I listened as if the Counseling Teacher’s voice had seized me by the throat.

‘That’s....’

Because I’d realized it—chillingly similar....

...To me, not long ago.

‘They’ve become aware.’

That sensation I’d felt when I came to my senses inside the 613th May 4.

The Counseling Teacher was speaking it out loud.

“But the situation is different, so, yeah.... I burned to death.”

“Get back!”
