“Damn it.”

Footsteps turned urgent.

“Evacuate! Seal the platform. Seolpi, talisman!”

“Seal?”

In that instant.

The atmosphere sank into something bizarrely, chillingly still.

“Right. That was it. ...Sealing.”

...!

I heard them murmur, as if possessed.

“After I burned to death inside there, I slipped out through the door and remained on the platform. Because that’s what I am. And for four days I was trapped, listening to citizens kill themselves, struggling to scan the surface, the outside, any way I could. Thinking I’d beg anyone—anything—to help this city... but then.”

.......

“You started some strange rite.”

My heart tightened.

“When I looked closely, it was a sacrificial rite. I wondered what kind of sacrifice you were offering to stop this, what sacrifices and costs you were willing to pay to save my students—my clients—burning with worry and also filled with ecstasy, desperately waiting for its completion....”

“The sacrifice was my clients.”

“You put countless people living in this city onto the ritual ground and offered them up. No—not only people. You scraped up every living thing, down to a single blade of grass on the surface. And then, and then....”

.......

“May 4 began again.”

I noticed a flash of realization cutting into my dulled mind like lightning.

‘Ah.’

A day repeating... wasn’t part of a catastrophic supernatural disaster.

‘It was part of the sealing rite....’

Then the reason the day repeated was probably two things....

To stop the spread of infection.

And....

To keep reviving and supplying living sacrifices to maintain the seal.

Se-gwang Special City was, like a self-generating facility, offering itself up as the sacrifice—supplying itself every day to sustain the city’s sealing....

Trapped on May 4.

‘Ah.’

A sickening cold settled into my body.

It wasn’t because I’d lost blood.

“So,”

.......

“My clients, again—everyone living gathered in this Gwangjin....”

The Counseling Teacher’s voice rang hollow across the platform.

...I couldn’t hear the agents anymore.

As if they’d already blocked off the collapsed platform with talismans and the like, the screams and noise and voices I’d been hearing a moment ago vanished—as if someone had flipped a switch.

It became quiet.

But.

A strange, bubbling presence pressed down.

‘...No.’

“So they’ll die miserably every day. And I’ll keep watching them, and I’ll burn to death. You used these hundreds of thousands, these millions of lives as bait instead of rescue. You lied to me with empty words, with sweet words on your tongue, and you....”

Thud.

I slammed my head down.

“......!”

Thud, thud....

“Agent....”

I let my head go, limp, where it struck the concrete.

I didn’t have the strength to move anymore—no energy, no force, nothing....

“Just, just a moment....”

Soon, I felt it as a wobbling vibration—someone moving alone.

Hands clearing the debris above me.

It took a painfully long time.

The sense of someone barely shifting something heavy—movements that seemed about to fail but didn’t break—continued, and then....

Snap.

At last, the chunk of cement that had been crushing down on my head was gone.

Light poured in, but....

I couldn’t see.

“.......”

Had I injured my head wrong?

All I felt, over my fogged mind, was someone moving a hand as if gently patting my head.

“It’s okay. It’ll be okay....”

With their one remaining hand, they soothed my head the way you would stroke an injured person’s hair.

Drip, drip. The sensation of hot droplets—like blood—falling onto my forehead made my head throb....

“...Did you come from outside to help me?”

I barely twitched a finger.

“Thank you....”

“.......”

“This city has been abandoned. Far from protecting it... I’m just a fox being offered up alongside it.”

A whisper trembling with rage and despair.

Splat.

Something collapses.

No.

‘As a counselor... you have to speak.’

I barely raised my arm and tapped them. Thump.

“Ah! Ah....”

“.......”

They tried to prop my upper body up and lift it onto their own leg.

.......

Splat.

Splat.

“A counselor can see off a client after the counseling ends.”

A laugh.

“Of course, an ordinary counselor couldn’t. Haha....”

Splat.

The feel of the hand on my head disappeared.

A sensation of something melting, sliding down my cheek, and dripping away onto the floor to vanish.

Now I heard only a voice in the air.

The Counseling Teacher’s faint voice.

“Before your breath cuts out and you’re placed on the sacrificial ritual ground—before you’re trapped in this May 4... I’ll send you back to where you originally were. Back to your workplace.”

Click.

I heard the sound of a doorknob being pulled.

And right after that, the feel of the floor supporting my back vanished—like a door had opened behind it.

My body fell into it.

To the other side of the door the counselor opened.

“Goodbye.”

I felt the touch of a small furry animal’s paws wrapping around my waist, and together we fell through empty space.......

.......

“Go back in peace. My client.”

That was the last thing.

‘......Ah.’

I slowly regained consciousness.

The pain throughout my body was gone like a lie, but an oddly sunken heaviness remained in my heart.

Newly learned facts filled my head.

The Pleasant Research Institute, the true nature of the disaster, the sealing rite.......

Hoyuwon.

At the very end, Hoyuwon had seemed to almost lose their shape and collapse.

Was it because they’d tried to send me out by using some sacred—or wicked—power in their true form, rather than the safe identity of a “counselor”...?

I thought of the small fox’s touch that still felt like it should be clinging to my waist, but the sensation was already gone.

‘...Get up.’

This wasn’t the time to sink into stray thoughts.

I got out, but the other three people... right, I didn’t know what happened to Section Chief Lee Jaheon and the Deputy and Bronze Agent.

I hoped we all woke up outside together.

The wall of the security isolation room in Daydream Inc’s basement.

What, what is this... what is....

-I’ll send you back to your workplace.

In that hand was my contract—wait.

My form melted—no.

It doesn’t melt, it doesn’t melt! I’m human! Endure, endure....

I have to stand up and find the security team suit—no, refuse....

What?

Director Cheongdallae looked at me.

The clear danger, the overwhelming violence—someone who slaughtered hundreds of thousands without hesitation, burned down the institute, and took the dream incubator.

A being who thought only of their own profit.

An evil that was terrifying because it was understandable, peering at me with delighted interest.

A cheerful voice.

No—then I realized I had no body.

I tried to pass through the wall and shoot out into the corridor.

Ah.

“The walls of a secured area isolate the rank of what’s inside from what’s outside—even conceptually. Otherwise how could we isolate supernatural phenomena?”

“Clever, but unfortunate. Once you’re properly dressed, I’ll have to discipline you. How about killing a child?”

Please.

...I couldn’t do anything but approach the cabinet.

The hand opening the cabinet door trembled. Security team employee 130666—strangely exerting physical force without a body—stuck their head into the cabinet, and....

.......

I heard the sound of a bell.

-Found it.

I was dragged into the cabinet.

A tremendous pressure wrapped around me and yanked upward like I was being pulled out, and before long I was torn free from Daydream Inc’s security zone.

An alarm blared behind me.

But in an instant, like prey being seized by the nape, I was transferred—caught in something enormous.

Somewhere.

“Podo.”

“.......”

“Podo,”

I opened my eyes.

I turned my head and looked at my arm.

It was there.

I looked at my hand.

It was there.

It moved.

I had a human body....

“.......”

But just now, just now...!

“Huhk, huhh...!”

“It’s okay.”

Panting, I tried to steady myself to the rhythm of the sensation patting my back. Breathe, breathe....

“It’s all over. You’re safe....”

I composed myself.

“.......”

“Podo.”

I lifted my head.

...Agent Choi.

The Black Turtle Team 1 agent with the pale face gave a grin and set the ritual bell they’d been holding off to the side.

Blood spattered from the bell.

A trembling voice spoke.

“Good to see you. ...It’s been 293 days.”

Chapter 347

I swallowed.

Agent Choi looked unwell.

Even though the hope and joy on his smiling face—at a teammate returning—was unmistakable....

Deep fatigue showed through underneath.

“How’s your body.”

“.......”

“When you’ve been lying down forever and you just get up, your muscles are all gone and it’s seriously rough, right? Ha—your senior’s been there too. Take it easy. Easy.”

Following his banter, I carefully pushed myself up.

My arms and legs trembled, but for someone who’d been asleep for 293 days, it wasn’t bad. Maybe I... No. Stop thinking about that.

What mattered was that I’d escaped Se-gwang Special City—and Director Cheong’s grasp—for the moment.

Probably thanks to this man.

I tried to hide my still-shaking hands as I turned to thank him, but the surrounding scene made me freeze.

“...Where even is this?”

“Somewhere with good air and good water.”

It looked like the inside of a natural cave.

A small space between jagged rocks, like a hidden shelter carved out inside.

In one section of the rock wall, five-colored cloths were draped like a ritual shrine, concealing something....

And beside me, I saw the sleeping body of Bronze Agent Ryu Jaegwan.

“.......”

I looked between the neatly arranged bedding where Bronze Agent lay sleeping and my own place, then turned back to Agent Choi.

“...We definitely entered Se-gwang Special City through the Fox Counseling Office.”

“I diverted you.”

“...!!”

Then Agent Choi suddenly brightened again.

“Anyway, that was perfect timing just now, right? This is what the top ace of Black Turtle Team 1 does. Podo. See? I told you I’d save you right on time.”

“.......”

A bad feeling crept up, swallowing everything.

I glanced to the side.

Agent Choi tried to cover it like he was casually clearing it away, but the ritual bell was still there.

And the bloodstain that had burst out and run from it.

“...Why did blood spatter from the bell?”

“It’s nothing.”

“Something like that.”

“With a good method.”

“Agent.”

My voice shook.

“What, exactly, did you offer to whom?”

“.......”

Agent Choi stared at me for a moment....

Then burst out laughing.

“What are you talking about! Hey, look.”

He rolled up his sleeve and showed me an arm packed with scars. He rolled up his pant leg too, then pressed at his body.

...He looked fine.

“I’m fine, yeah?”

Then he shrugged.

“Don’t say stuff people can misunderstand~ You’ll make it sound like a cool agent like me, who thinks about citizens day and night, is some addict gambling their body away at a casino.”

Then what, exactly, did you...?

“Just rest for now. Eat... and then we’ll talk.”

“.......”

“Hm?”

In Agent Choi’s gaunt, smiling face, I saw something desperate.

I remembered Bronze Agent still asleep beside me.

And I thought.

What it must have been like for him, alone, for the last 293 days.

“.......”

In the end, I nodded slowly.

Agent Choi grinned wide.

“Come on, eat up.”

The food laid out was egg porridge and side dishes.

The side dishes looked like frozen or packaged food, but the egg porridge tasted freshly made, like it had been cooked somewhere just now....

[It looks like a spread someone scraped together with real effort, given how little they had.]

I ate as earnestly as I could. Agent Choi’s face grew a little brighter, steadier.

So I could ask.

“Agent. While I was asleep... what happened?”

“.......”

Agent Choi opened his mouth.

“There’s one piece of good news and one piece of bad news. Which do you want first?”

“...Uh,”

“No, forget it. I’ll just tell you.”

He waved a hand and looked at me.

A calm warmth settled in his eyes.

“Podo. Do you remember the train shelter?”

“Of course... I remember.”

Because I fell there.

While I was falling—and even while I was going through things I don’t want to remember—I still remembered the train, slicing through the far-off air, leaving toward outside the city.

Maybe it was better that it was empty air.

Maybe it ran in the “not-surface” sky by accident, and that was why, instead of crashing or stopping, it could safely use supernatural phenomena as fuel.

Like how the subway, separated from the surface, stayed comparatively intact.

But the fuel... both agents disappeared from the train.

Me and Bronze Agent.

Cold spread through me as I jerked my head up.

...No.

The people on that train—Mr. Yeong Eun who was aboard.......

“Podo.”

“.......”

“The train made it outside. And....”

“The people inside all survived.”

I stared at him like I’d been punched.

“You saved them.”

“...Really?”

“Really.”

“Everyone... got out safely?”

Agent Choi nodded.

“Yeah.”

“...!”

“Congratulations. Black Turtle Team 1 Agent Podo. ...You safely rescued 104 citizens from a catastrophic supernatural disaster.”

Ah.

A surge I couldn’t put into words hit my chest for the first time in ages.

A sense of accomplishment.

...Thank god.

It was such a relief.

[Congratulations. Friend!]

So much relief that it made breathing feel tight.

How did it happen? How did they keep the train running safely with an engine that used agents as fuel...?

“Was someone in the engineer’s cab?”

“Oh, that’s a pretty specific question.”

But Agent Choi answered without hesitation.

“From the reports, there were two. A middle-aged man with bruises all over his body.”

...The engineer.

“And... there was a young woman beside him, checking the controls and standing guard.”

......No way.

“Agent Parkha.”

Ah.

Ah...!

It was Go Yeong Eun.

If I could be judged an agent, then Go Yeong Eun could too. Right...!

And since there wasn’t much distance left before the city border when they escaped, she must have made it into the cab safely.

“Is Go Yeong Eun okay?”

“She was hospitalized for a bit, but she was discharged healthy.”

“.......”

Thank god.

Yeong Eun got out of Se-gwang Special City....

It didn’t feel real. I wanted to see her.

And even if the conditions lined up like this, I needed to find out how the train managed to slip through the seal.

Then it hit me like lightning.

Because it was the subway...!

The subway people weren’t offered up. They weren’t included inside May 4.

They were an exception inside the seal’s structure.

So that’s why they could get out.

Everything clicked into place in my head at once, and relief surged again like a rush. Then....

“The rescued citizens were taken care of by the Bureau after Agent Parkha reported it.”

“.......”

“Podo?”

“Hm? Of course. When you’re rescued from a supernatural disaster, you go back after simple memory erasure. Even if you came out from inside a cognitive seal, the process isn’t any different.”

...Haa.

“They did contamination screening, confirmed it wasn’t infectious. Restored their identities, and they all got out.”

“...I see.”

Agent Choi answered without the slightest hesitation. He didn’t seem to realize what I was worried about.

That was what made it chilling.

...What if “someone” who planned the sealing rite had intervened somehow?

But Agent Choi’s tone turned a little playful.

“Oh, there was one thing nobody expected that made the procedure pass quickly.”

“If it was something nobody expected...?”

Agent Choi looked at me.

More precisely, at the Good Friend doll placed neatly beside my bedding.

“Mmm~ The rescued citizens showed this cult-like tendency to fervently worship a pink rabbit plush?”

Ah.

“When we looked closer, it was supernatural phenomena. So that’s what the conclusion became.”

Agent Choi lightly slapped his knee.

“‘Those citizens were contaminated by a cult-like supernatural phenomenon related to a plush doll, and are presumed to have entered that phenomenon and indirectly bypassed the seal’!”

“.......”

“Seems like that’s how it was judged. Processed as a special case.”

My god.

[Ha ha, for viewers waiting in trust of this Brown, there is always a gift-like surprise!]

“Well, they said the memories related to supernatural phenomena were cleaned up before they returned to society, but the rabbit plush enthusiasm apparently remained?”

Aaaah....

I didn’t know whether to laugh or cry....

But I was so insanely relieved that I just nodded.

Then a bitter smile rose on Agent Choi’s face.

“The problem is... a few of those citizens went missing again as soon as they returned.”

“To some counseling office.”

......!

“Yeah. It was done by the being you call Director Ho. They heard that someone got out from the special city.”

Ah.

“.......”

Agent Choi’s pale blue eyes sank calmly.

“So I diverted you. I know what they’ll do with you if you leave you there.”

His voice trembled for a moment, then quickly steadied.

“Honestly, I wanted to divert your other... sleeping teammates too, but they’d separated the rooms completely.”

“.......”

“No. Absolutely not....”

How could I possibly demand, Why did you make that choice?

And this agent already knew I wouldn’t be able to say that....

“Right. It’s best not to see that dangerous director ever again, yeah? If you ever need to go back into the special city underground, you can use the well.”

“.......”

I couldn’t answer yes.

I already knew too much.

Even though he’d clearly noticed, Agent Choi deliberately forced his tone bright again.

“Oh, and there’s that. Agent Parkha seems like she’s trying to return to duty?”

“Yeah.”

A soft, low voice continued from behind.

“And you’ll see her soon, too. You can hear the detailed explanation about the train from her.”

...!

I sprang up and turned my head.

My head spun for a moment, but more than that, I saw a familiar face emerging from beyond the rocks.

“Agent Haegum!”

“Agent Podo.”

Agent Haegum’s expression, bright with delight at seeing me, twisted the moment she saw Agent Choi grinning beside me.

“You—!”

“Hahaha, hey, sis, it worked out! Choi pulled off another one, right?”

Agent Haegum, who’d been striding over like she was about to smack him, let out a sigh and lowered her hand.

“...I came when I got the message. Agent Podo, I’m glad you woke up safely.”

“Thank you.”

Watching them, I felt a sudden doubt.

“Are you both... off duty?”

No—more than that.

Wasn’t Agent Choi the only one left in Black Turtle Team 1...?

Had he been operating alone?

Maybe he sensed my gaze, because Agent Choi gave a slight laugh and looked away.

“Black Turtle Team 1 is... being processed as all on leave.”

“...!”

“It’s my first time in my life taking leave that isn’t medical leave. But I was due for a break anyway. Hey, what’s with that face.”

Smiling, Agent Choi shook his head.

“No problem. The people who were on leave returned to Black Turtle Team 5. They took over our schedule completely and are working it now.”

Listening to him ramble about things like, “They said 4 sounded bad, so they insisted on 5 and even picked a number off on its own,” my chest felt heavy.

Only then did I realize this place wasn’t just somewhere to stash us while we slept—it was a base Agent Choi had been living in for months.

He even took leave and kept searching for a way to wake Bronze Agent and me....

Otherwise there was no way he could have pulled me out of Director Cheong’s grasp with timing that perfect.

And once I reached that thought, I made up my mind.

I took a deep breath.

“Aren’t you going to ask what I went through?”

“...Are you ready to talk?”

I think I am.

...About the truth I learned in Se-gwang Special City.

I was about to open my mouth, but I realized the two agents were tense in an odd way.

And I noticed another discomfort.

I’d felt it from the moment I opened my eyes, but I hadn’t dared to bring it to the surface... the wrongness.

Why aren’t they asking about everyone else’s whereabouts...?

They didn’t ask why the others besides me hadn’t woken up.

Even if they knew I would speak first if it was urgent, wouldn’t their position—waiting outside for almost a year—be different?

Wouldn’t they be desperate?

At the very least, you’d think they’d urgently ask whether Bronze Agent was okay, alive, safe—whether they’d even seen him. But they kept dodging the topic.

...Like they wanted to spare me, even a little more.

“.......”

Spare me from what?

-There’s one piece of good news and one piece of bad news. Which do you want first?

The news about the train survivors being rescued was good news, no matter how you heard it.

Then....

“Agent.”

“.......”

“What was the bad news...?”

“.......”

“Agent?”

A pained look flickered across Agent Choi’s face, then got smoothed away with practiced ease into a calm, capable agent’s expression.

“Stay calm.”

Agent Haegum spoke.

“Agent Podo. Good and bad always come stuck together like two sides of a coin.”

“...What do you mean?”

Agent Haegum looked straight into my eyes.

“Don’t panic. Listen slowly.”

.......

“The special city’s seal is breaking.”

Information poured into my head, turning it white.

“Looks like the train became the trigger. The moment more than a hundred people escaped from inside the special city, something in the sealing rite’s conditions... seems to have gone out of alignment. A crack is forming.”

Wait.

“People are keeping it quiet inside, but this damned Choi bastard pulled something dangerous... anyway, he clawed the information out.”

Agent Haegum nodded toward the ritual space concealed by the five-colored cloth.

“He kept watching the seal from here. How it twisted, and when it would lose its effect.”

.......

“How much time is left?”

“300 days.”

Ha.

So we had to find a method somehow within that. As my mouth went dry at the sudden time limit—

I realized Agent Haegum was still speaking.

“The collapse began with a 300-day grace period from the day the train escaped....”

Wait, wait....

“Today, 293 of those days have passed.”

.......

.......

“Seven.”

My breath stopped.

“In a week, the special city’s sealing rite will collapse.”

I stared blankly at Agent Haegum.

“Then a catastrophic disaster will be released onto this land.”

Chapter 348

I wanted to snap back, “What did you say?”—but the truth was, my head had already understood what Agent Haegum meant.

It felt like something in my brain had burst from the shock.

...The sealing rite is collapsing?

So the disaster in Se-gwang Special City... is going to be completely released?

And that—

There’s a week left.

An enormous fear surged up on reflex.

After coming back with my skin still remembering the “disaster’s” true shape, panic with almost physical, crackling edges raised goosebumps across my mind.

Then....

It’s the end.

What happens?

Even though I knew the seal’s horrific nature, even though I’d seen the Se-gwang Special City citizens’ suffering with my own eyes, my face was probably drained white.

Because in a few days, that scene would arrive across the entire country.

Powerless—no, wait.

I turned to Agent Haegum.

There was no way I was the only one feeling this fear.

“Does the Bureau... not know?”

If they did, they wouldn’t have wasted the last 300 days doing nothing. They would have built some kind of countermeasure.

The problem was, if they had built a countermeasure, these two agents wouldn’t be talking about a catastrophic disaster detonating in a week in this tone.

...Are they keeping it secret?

Agent Haegum frowned slightly.

“You know. There are barely any agents or officials in the Bureau who even know about that special city.”

......!

Right. They erased all the agents’ memories and made the city cognitively unrecognizable.

In the process, Blue Dragon Team vanished outright, and the organization was even restructured as if it had always been that way.

But.

“Still, someone who knows would know.”

“.......”

Looking into Agent Haegum’s eyes, I realized who she meant.

Whoever led the sealing rite.

Someone who pushed that chilling plan—making May 4, the disaster day, repeat infinitely, using endlessly dying people as fuel to keep the sealing rite running.

Someone higher up would definitely know.

“...But from what we’ve pieced together, me and that bastard Choi... there hasn’t been any special movement inside the Bureau over the last few months. If they were taking measures, it should have affected the sealing rite. The fact that it hasn’t means even that isn’t happening.”

“That’s....”

Wait.

One horrifying assumption brushed through my mind.

If they weren’t moving now, then—

“...Are they waiting until the seal disappears completely?”

“.......”

“And then thinking they’ll perform the sealing rite again right after it disappears?”

“Yeah.”

“...!”

“If you have to seal it anyway, it’s a method that’s already been proven. I think there’s a high chance they’ll choose that over uncertain reinforcement.”

Ha.

“The only problem is... where they’ll get the ‘sacrifice.’”

Agent Haegum seemed tense only at the variable in that question itself.

But I already knew the answer.

...Just offer up the freed Se-gwang Special City citizens all over again.

This is insane.

My scalp prickled.

I opened my mouth urgently.

I have to say this....

“Agent—about the sealing rite... have you already talked about it with the other agent?”

About human sacrifice.

I tried to keep it vague, but the next moment I met Agent Choi’s eyes and knew.

“Why are you circling around it. Podo. We already heard.”

......!

A dry voice came out of that bitter smile, tired.

But there was almost no anger or confusion.

...As if he’d already chewed it to pieces over and over, calm.

“Ah. Worried about being tracked? Then it’s fine to talk here. They can’t chase us this close under the Tiger General’s resting place.”

And he added,

“...It’s a place the Bureau doesn’t even know about in the first place.”

“.......”

“We tried everything to pull only living citizens out while keeping the existing seal itself... but we just couldn’t interfere with the rite at all.”

His blue irises fixed on me.

“All the way up to this point—seven days before the seal ends.”

“.......”

His head must have been at the breaking point.

His direct junior and the ex-spy were missing from a catastrophic disaster and still couldn’t wake, the organization he’d given his life to served some chilling secret, Hoyuwon could do something unpredictable at any moment....

Looking at those hollow eyes that had clearly never rested even on leave, my own chest turned cold.

A crisis right in front of us.

I’m losing it.

Procedures and loose ends whipped through my head—Director Ho, the security team’s status, the researchers at Twilight Station, the Blood Broadcast Bureau rental fee, the lizard body stored at Section Chief Lee Jaheon’s place....

A 293-day blank, choking the throat.

But all of it would become meaningless if we couldn’t stop the catastrophic disaster coming in a week.

And yet re-sealing Se-gwang Special City... I couldn’t bring myself to even consider that as anything but evil.

Either way was hell for someone.

...At this point, how much does Director Ho know?

No. Focus.

If I couldn’t change what was coming, I had to move faster.

I had to contain this somehow.

“Agent Podo, be care—”

“I’m fine.”

I stood up without support. My legs shook.

My head cleared.

The first thing I needed was....

“I want to meet Yeong Eun—Agent Parkha.”

The incident where the seal started breaking.

I needed to reconstruct the circumstances from the person who’d been the center of gravity when the train shelter escaped.

Fast.

Go Yeong Eun arrived at Agent Choi’s hideout under Agent Haegum’s guidance less than half a day later.

“Soleum!”

Yeong Eun really was fine.

Instead of that haggard padded jacket she’d worn in the Se-gwang Special City subway, she looked neat and composed, like when I first saw her at the company, wearing casual clothes without any special processing.

...Which also meant all her exposed body parts were present.

-So the body parts Yeong Eun lost at Body Casino....

-We got them back from Body Casino after the escape.

-...!

-Yeah.

“I can’t believe you woke up. Really, really thank god....”

-Not even two weeks after escaping, she called Choi and asked him to tell her the “escape-by-dying method” we used to enter the special city again.

-In the end, she went back in as soon as she recovered. And at Body Casino, which had reopened for business, she retrieved every part she’d lost.

-The only thing missing is her ring finger. They said someone else already took it from the pawnshop.

I watched Yeong Eun lift her left hand—missing its ring finger—and wipe her face, hiding a sigh of relief.

And I forced myself to speak.

“Yeong Eun. I should have come to see you, but with the situation right now, it was difficult—so I asked the agents to invite you here.”

“Of course it’s fine! No, if you just woke up, you must be exhausted—your muscles and everything would be gone....”

Yeong Eun’s eyes flickered with something strange when she realized I was in better shape than expected, but instead of asking directly, she quietly closed her mouth.

...Even there, I felt an odd flow of time.

“Yeong Eun, have you been doing okay? ...I heard you’ve been entering the special city subway.”

“I’m... better than before. I’m living with my aunt. My aunt... forgot everything that happened.”

Yeong Eun’s voice darkened for a moment, then returned.

“And honestly, one reason I’m trying to return to duty as an agent is so my memories won’t be erased.”

Ah.

“You might call it calculating... but losing my memories feels like losing myself.”

She added that right after the rescue, she’d deliberately kept saying she wanted to “return to duty,” implicitly delaying memory erasure.

“And afterward I brushed off getting my body parts back as being thanks to an item. ...It sounds kind of extreme, doesn’t it? This isn’t Daydream Inc, it’s the Disaster Management Bureau.”

“No. I completely understand.”

Especially from Yeong Eun’s perspective—someone who’d been trying to win a wish without even knowing why she’d forgotten, after losing her memories of her family in Se-gwang Special City.

And....

Once I reached that thought, my mouth wouldn’t move.

I’d planned to say it again only when all three were together, because I didn’t have the confidence to speak it “normally” after telling the two agents.

Damn it.

But we didn’t have time, and ruin was coming.

“Yeong Eun.”

“Yes?”

“There’s good... news, and bad news.”

A bitter smile appeared at the corner of Agent Choi’s mouth, then vanished.

But as he listened to what I said next, even that bitter smile disappeared.

“The good news is that the other people may have a chance to wake up safely, too.”

“That’s... that’s really good news!”

A flash of joy lit Yeong Eun’s voice, then she forced herself calm.

Agent Choi’s eyes were on me too.

“Deputy Eun Haje and Ryu Jaegwan... and Section Chief Lee Jaheon—are they alive? The three of you disappeared from the train too, so we thought you... fell together.”

I nodded slowly. A cold anger flickered across Yeong Eun’s face.

“...That conductor shoved you, right?”

“.......”

“I knew it. So that’s what it was. That insane bastard....”

She said she didn’t regret hitting him at all, then steadied her breathing and continued.

“But still—thank god you were alive. ...We couldn’t find any trace of you four in the subway, no matter how hard we searched.”

“.......”

“Then you were on the surface?”

“Yes. ...It was completely unexpected, but there were people living on the surface of Se-gwang Special City.”

I met Yeong Eun’s eyes.

“And they were dying every day.”

“......What?”

The air turned cold.

“...What do you mean?”

I told her.

About Se-gwang Special City’s catastrophe, repeating May 4.

“...!!”

And....

“...That’s... how you seal the disaster?”

“.......”

“By repeating a single day every day, so no matter how much time passes the disaster can’t leave that city’s radius—people are just... dying every day?”

From Yeong Eun’s blank words, emotion slowly began to boil.

“Then my mom and dad...?”

“Yeong Eun....”

“Now, how many years has it been... I—”

She sucked in a sharp breath. I rushed to her side to pat her back, but Yeong Eun pushed my hand away.

...!

“.......”

Her head dropped low. For a long time, she didn’t answer.

Then, slowly, I heard her murmuring.

“...You’re saying this is real? This is reality? Really... that happened?”

“...It might only be my guess—”

“You’re always right! Every deduction you made was right every time! Why would you say something like that now.......”

She steadied her breathing again.

A stabbing silence sank into the cave.

I looked at Yeong Eun’s clenched fist.

It was trembling, drained white.

I saw Agent Choi slowly rise and move soundlessly to her side.

He—and Agent Haegum too—were pale, cold. But in front of the person whose family was involved, they didn’t show their shock.

After I barely steadied my own breathing, I asked him in a small, coded voice.

“...Did you know?”

About Yeong Eun.

“You’re asking if I didn’t know the seal would break in seven days?”

......!

“Of course I knew. Even after I got out, my ability to hear ‘rumors related to the special city’ didn’t disappear. Honestly, that rumor was why I kept trying to go into the subway.”

Yeong Eun’s voice cracked.

“When I think about it.”

“...Yes.”

“If we just let that seal break... isn’t there a chance my mom and dad could be alive when it breaks?”

...!

“May 4 keeps repeating. So if the timing matches, and it breaks at a time when lots of people are still alive... then wouldn’t they get fixed there while alive?”

[Oh, an interesting guess!]

Instead, my blood felt like it was turning cold.

There was no way it would go that conveniently, that kindly—but I couldn’t bring myself to refute her.

“...Yeong Eun. In that scenario, within days this entire country would—”

“Then we run away. As fast as we can. If we go to the other side of the earth, it might be safe...! And,”

Even while panting, Yeong Eun’s voice was icy.

“Why should I have to care about any of that?”

“...!”

“Let it fall apart. Let them handle it themselves. I—sorry....”

She clenched her teeth.

“No, I’m not sorry at all. ...I only want to apologize for snapping at you.”

“.......”

Agent Haegum lowered her sword.

Then she slowly patted Yeong Eun’s back.

“Honestly, you don’t even have to apologize for that. I feel the same way....”

“.......”

I could tell Yeong Eun was gritting her teeth and crying soundlessly.

Not tears of sadness—tears of agony and rage.

...Suppressed fury, like the Counseling Teacher.

I forced down the emotion rising in my own throat.

...Is there really no way?

Was Yeong Eun’s idea only ever going to remain a personal plan, a desperate hope? Or was there—.

.......

Wait.

“What if we end it first?”

“...Pardon?”

“If we end the disaster before the seal breaks... it might be possible.”

“Agent Podo!”

“Wait.”

But Agent Choi looked at me immediately.

A strange light turned in his eyes.

“So if the disaster ends right before the seal breaks... the citizens might survive while alive.”

“Yes.”

It felt like fire had flared inside my head.

Now I knew how to go from the subway to the outside.

Walk along the maintenance tracks.

If I stepped onto the surface of Se-gwang Special City on a rewound morning.

If it was some extremely early point, before the city itself became completely contaminated.

Then a termination attempt... could be tried...!

And I already had a “method” I’d been preparing—after getting a hint.

-Then somehow divert the dream incubator Daydream Inc copied

Install it in what’s left of the Pleasant Research Institute facility

The potion manufacturer I’d diverted from Daydream Inc.

And the ones I entrusted it to....

The researchers.

.......

The experience of May 4 on disaster day meshed with the contents of that note in my head.

It was almost there. But....

“Even if the odds are low, isn’t it something we can try?”

I lifted my head.

Yeong Eun was catching her breath.

In her eyes, a conflict flashed like sparking wires—fighting not to hope, calculating fiercely.

“Yeong Eun.”

“.......”

“Have you ever visited Twilight Station in the special city underground? There’s a resort pop-up store there, and inside....”

“...You mean Mr. Heo Un, right?”

“Yes. And there should have been Daydream Inc researchers there.......”

But I saw the answer in Yeong Eun’s expression.

“They weren’t... there?”

“No.”

......!!

“Wait.”

Before my blood could turn completely cold, Yeong Eun’s calm voice cut in.

“I’ve heard a ‘rumor’ that Director Ho... secured the Daydream Inc employees who went missing in the subway.”

...!

“So I think the current project team... has them secured somewhere in the subway stations.”

.......

“...Do you need those people... for the termination attempt?”

I nodded.

“Then... let’s go meet them.”

“...!”

“Before it’s too late, let’s go see Director Ho.”

Disaster-day until arrival: D-6.

Chapter 349

I took a breath.

Going to see Director Ho wasn’t difficult in practical terms.

I was part of Director Ho’s project team, and since I often worked alone, the director had given me the authority to come and go freely within his domain.

Unlike the agents who’d siphoned off Director Ho’s sleeping project team members, that “authority” was still alive for me.

In a physical form, at that.

Fox Counseling Office

"......"

I took out that warmly colored nameplate from inside my tattoo and held it up.

"...Are you going?"

"Yes."

The two agents decided to stay behind instead of accompanying me, so as not to provoke Hoyuwon.

...The persuasion process was a little oppressive, though.

[You really were a persistent one. Whew!]

As I walked the rugged path out of Agent Choi’s hideout, I remembered Brown pouring out “jokes” that were really complaints disguised as humor, and I felt a faint smile brush the corner of my mouth.

The tension eases a little.

Even so, when I think about what’s about to happen, my hands are still stiff.

"......"

A rural road in Gangwon Province.

I hung the nameplate on the front door of one of the scattered empty houses, then pulled at the old door.

Creeeak.

Sunlight poured in.

A calm, soft, meticulously prepared interior revealed itself.

The Fox Counseling Office.

A warm space in a neatly arranged waiting room, prepared to receive clients.

When I first set foot here, I’d suspected even this comfortable atmosphere was part of an urban legend’s gimmick.

Even after that, every time I came in, the cozy atmosphere made me wary first.

But not now.

That’s....

Maybe it was because I’d felt—truly felt—how sincere this being was about counseling, about clients.

Even with just a single day of May 4 that repeats forever.

"...There’s no presence. Should we go in?"

"Yes."

I stepped inside carefully.

"Hoyuwon."

There was no answer.

But I kept going further in.

Past the table set with tea and snacks, toward the inner door.

Long-Term Inpatient Room

A door with a placard that read, “Rest space for inpatient stays.”

The very place where we’d entered the Se-gwang Special City subway.

I carefully took hold of the doorknob.

In that moment.

"Noru...?"

...!

"Chief Badger."

When I turned around, a big man with a warm impression was standing there as if he’d been there all along.

A face I hadn’t seen in a very long time... Chief Park Minseong.

That familiar face stared at me with trembling eyes, then hurried over.

"You’re awake, you’re safe! Thank god...!"

Had he recovered from the contamination to some extent? Relief washed through me as I looked at Chief Park Minseong, whole-limbed and able to speak normally.

...In this moment, the 293-day gap almost didn’t feel like it had been all bad.

"This is... ah, it’s Ms. Goral. You came together?"

"Yes.... Hello, Chief."

Go Yeong Eun greeted him gently.

But in the face I caught in a glance, I saw a strange expression flicker past.

...Why?

A bad feeling pricked at me, but Go Yeong Eun quickly smoothed her expression away, leaving only a polite smile.

Instead, the welcoming light faded from Chief Park Minseong’s face, and tension settled in.

"This place... no, you two should get out first. While Director Ho isn’t here...."

"It’s fine. I have something to say to him."

"Th-that’s not a good idea either.... If you just woke up, you might not have heard the news, but... Director Ho, his condition seems to be getting stranger and stranger."

Chief Park Minseong lowered his voice.

"Almost half the people who went in for counseling went missing. And not long ago... someone high up from the company’s HR team came, got transferred to the security zone, and never came back out."

"......"

"It’s getting harder and harder to have anything like a real conversation, and now... it barely feels like he’s even a person."

"Even so, I have to talk."

Chief Park Minseong’s face seemed to harden, then melted into an oddly gentle expression.

"Why are you being so stubborn... Listen to the teacher’s wor—, no, no...!"

...!

With his face covered, Chief Park Minseong panted, then jerked his head up and spoke.

In a slightly blank tone.

"Hey, I’ll go in."

"...Pardon?"

"Into the Se-gwang Special City subway."

...?!

"Th-the project, yeah, I’m exploring too. The subway stations... I’m off duty right now? So I was here for a bit, but it sounded like someone woke up inside. Yeah? Ah, it’s coming out."

The long-term inpatient room door opened.

"...!"

Coming out of it was... a bone-dry, faded, shaggy-haired man.

A man still wearing an old security-uniform jacket.

J3.

"...Mr. J3."

"I’m going."

And Chief Park Minseong, as if fleeing, went through the door the security chief had come out of and into the long-term inpatient room, then shut it.

Thud.

......

The security chief, who’d been staring blankly after that, turned his head toward me and opened his mouth.

"Hey, want some tea...?"

"...Pardon?"

He fumbled as if trying to take out the snacks on the desk with clumsy hands, and I hurriedly stopped him.

"It’s fine. Um, I think it would be good if you had some, Mr. J3. It looks like you just finished working."

"Yeah...."

He kept glancing at me, then quietly offered a few of the snacks toward me.

So it was his way of showing he was glad....

Still, it was strange—he accepted my being awake as if it were the most natural thing in the world.

And because of that, the words slipped out of me.

"Just now, what Chief Badger said...."

"Ah."

The security chief’s slow reply came.

"Badger... um....... He keeps wanting to stay in the subway...?"

......!

"As I thought...."

I heard Go Yeong Eun murmur.

When I turned my head, our eyes met.

"To be honest, I’ve secretly looked in on Director Ho’s project team inside the subway before. And back then... everyone was trying to leave, but I saw him trying to stay behind alone."

"......"

Suddenly, I remembered what happened when I explored Brightlight Library with Chief Park Minseong.

-Um, should I just stay here?

...That anxious voice.

Now I felt like I understood why, in that subway, contaminated people were relatively more lucid.

Based on “that day, May 4,” people who’d been sane and uninfected on the surface might have regained the mental framework they had back then, influenced by the distortions happening aboveground.

And maybe it had been strengthened further by the influence of a “world without urban legends.”

What mattered was that Chief Park Minseong had become obsessed with that effect.

"Yeah.... In the subway... you don’t get tossed around by contamination.... It’s quiet, so it’s nice...."

"Even so... leaving him like that."

"Um, in the end it’s something he has to bear himself...?"

"......"

"He has to... regulate it himself and accept it...."

In that calm, almost cold analysis, a faint outline of his old self seemed to surface....

Even though this man had been contaminated long before “May 4,” and stayed that way even in the subway, the wolf squad leader I’d seen at Brightlight Library flashed in my mind.

"...So you’ve kept working on the project."

"Yeah......"

"Is your work—your condition—alright?"

The security chief rubbed at his cap.

"Right.... Me, well... it can’t really get... worse...."

"......"

Somehow, it left a bitter taste.

Still, the fact he hadn’t been under Director Cheong made me exhale in genuine relief.

I swallowed the bitterness and asked the real question.

"I heard the project team is meeting the researchers in the subway and, um... keeping them safe. Do you know who that is?"

"It’s me...."

...?!

"Y-you mean you’re the one protecting them, Mr. J3?"

"More than protecting... finding and capturing...?"

"......"

"I watched them...."

So that’s... what it turned into.

If anything, that’s a relief....

At least the security chief wouldn’t deliberately harm them or try to take revenge on the researchers.

If anything, it felt more like Gwak Jegang would have picked a fight... no, with that temperament, he probably would’ve ignored it and moved on. Would that be fine?

Either way, some of the tension drains away.

"Are they okay?"

"More or less...? But right now... I’m off shift....... Dolphin... um, that person is watching them."

So Seongha was still being deployed....

I was about to ask immediately about Daydream Inc’s potion-manufacturing device the researchers would’ve been fixing.

But the security chief stared at me hard.

"Is that why he told you to come out...?"

"...Pardon?"

"He made me come out.... Looks like he found out... that you woke up...."

Who...?

But before I could reach the answer on my own, I heard it.

Click.

"...!"

I snapped my head around on reflex.

...Back there,

the counseling-room door was opening by itself.

Next client, please come in.

"......"

Client Kim Soleum, please come in.

"Mr. Noru,"

Go Yeong Eun broke into a cold sweat and grabbed my back as if trying to stop my sudden move.

I carefully removed her hand.

"...I’ll go ‘counsel’ and come back."

"......"

"Um, Mr. J3. Ms. Goral’s safety...."

The security chief’s yellow pupils looked down at Go Yeong Eun.

"It’s fine.... I’m here...."

Slowly, I nodded.

Then carefully—without hesitating—I stepped forward.

Into the counseling room.

......

......

A warm space.

A counseling room professionally designed to produce psychological effects.

But beyond the wide window—where the “counselor” used to appear with the same face as the client—there was only pitch-black darkness.

The simple, beautiful green backyard was gone.

A black night.

"......"

I walked forward and sat on the sofa in front of the window.

Behind me, I heard someone’s footsteps approach, then both hands settled on my shoulders as they spoke.

"Mr. Soleum."

......

"You’ve woken up. Very good. Now, it’s time to tell me what you’ve seen and heard. Before that, raise your hand—"

"Hello, Teacher."

The other person stopped.

"Have you been well? For nearly three hundred days, waiting without any word must have been very painful for you."

"......"

The hands lifted from my shoulders.

"What is your intention."

"I also heard you’ve been protecting the Se-gwang Special City citizens you counseled recently. When you met them in person—clients who’d forgotten everything—you must have felt both relief and anger."

"......"

"Did the Bureau tell you to say things like that?"

"No."

I spoke slowly.

"I haven’t met anyone from the Disaster Management Bureau on my way here. Except for the people who were part of the project."

"Ah, those agents who siphoned off the sleeping project people...."

"But it was strange."

"What was?"

"This Fox Counseling Office isn’t just a place. Isn’t it you yourself?"

The other person’s presence went still.

"Under a powerful obsession with maintaining your identity as a counselor, you’re maintaining this space, aren’t you?"

"That,"

"And yet, taking something out of here would be difficult without your tacit permission. Teacher."

"...!"

"Back then, you would’ve been startled by your own impulse to let that agent slide. ...But unable to act, you sent them away in an instant, didn’t you?"

"Who can say."

"Wasn’t it confusing—where that impulse came from? Why you feel such regret for your clients, why regular counseling deepens that feeling until you want to protect that person by hiding them from the outside... Have you ever thought about it?"

"Right now."

A low, chilling voice came.

"Are you trying to counsel me in reverse?"

"Maybe."

I forced a laugh.

"I’m imitating you, Teacher."

"...What?"

"Yes. I’m imitating the you I experienced. A counselor who stayed in one area for a very long time, grew attached to the people there, and tried to protect those clients."

"What you’re describing is...."

"Do you remember? When you counseled me for the first time."

I looked at the wide window.

"I came into this counseling room contaminated by the Hungry Hangman Darkness, carrying a training manual. And you... counseled me as me, from outside the window."

And then....

"When the counseling ended, I saw a small fox run into the garden."

"......"

"Was that you as well?"

"Darkness has many facets. But I don’t understand what this conversation is supposed to mean. Are you trying to stall for time?"

"No. There’s something I want to tell you, Teacher."

-Mr. Soleum.

Why did Hoyuwon use such extreme formality—only with me?

That....

As the pieces clicked into place, a sudden swell of emotion rose in my throat, and I opened my mouth.

"Teacher... when you came to your senses outside Se-gwang Special City, weren’t you also in the form of a small fox?"

"......"

"And when you came out, didn’t it feel like you came out with someone?"

"You,"

"You came out pushing someone bigger than you, but when you came to your senses, weren’t you not alone?"

"......"

“It couldn’t have been any other way.”

Because....

“We went to different points in time.”

“.......”

“You woke up a few years ago, on the afternoon of May 4—the day the disaster began.

I woke up yesterday.”

I turned my head.

And met Hoyuwon’s eyes, his expression gone.

—I’ll push you out.... I’ll attach the small fox.

Like letting you slip out through the counseling room’s back door, just a little.

The Counseling Teacher.

The small fragment he had sent with me to see me off was right before my eyes.

“Thank you, Teacher.”

I held out my hand.

“Thanks to you... I made it out safely.”

The Fox Counseling Office counselor stared at my hand with trembling eyes.

Then, slowly, they shook it.
